## R5. Round 5: rooms first, with an optimised fallback

§0 to §24 are round 4's design of kept slots. Round 5 keeps them as the mechanics of one option of the fallback (R5.7) and changes them
where R5 says so; R5 is what round 5 proposes.

### R5.1 What round 5 answers, and its frame

- **The owner's reframing (2026-11-07).** Each session today opens the whole repository as a fresh worktree and removes it afterwards. That
  helps nothing and, over time, wears the disk. The purpose is to end that churn: write only what changes. Build time is a second gain, not
  the reason.
- **Daoris is generic.** The repositories it drives use any stack: .NET, native C++, Python, Java and Gradle, Rust, Go, plain documents. So no
  code names a stack's folders: a repository declares them, and what it does not declare is treated as a fresh checkout treats it.
- **What git leaves unmanaged.** Git already manages the tracked files: one object store for every tree, a switch that writes only what
  differs. The ignored files are what nobody manages: dependencies, build intermediates, runnable outputs, local configuration, scratch.
  Today each session makes them again, copies them or deletes them whole, and R5.2 measures them at 93% of a session's bytes here. Round 5
  manages them by class (R5.5), under every way a session gets its files.
- **The frame, the owner's decision.** The central way is the **room** (R5.3): a session that holds a room works in place, in the
  repository's checkout, and needs no tree of its own. A repository, or a path, with no room yet gets the **fallback** (R5.7): its ignored
  content managed by class, its dependencies from a store, and a kept tree switched in place, or a copy-on-write clone where a Dev Drive
  exists. Today's fresh worktree stays only as the last resort. Rooms are adopted gradually (R5.4).
- **The read of round 4** is answered in §25. This repository's own build fix, the commit stamped into every assembly, is its own row
  (R5.8), apart from the generic design.

### R5.2 Measured: what a session writes

Measured on 2026-11-07, on Windows 11 (build 26300, NTFS), the .NET SDK 10.0.402, git 2.53.0.windows.3 and Node 24.19, with other sessions
building on the machine, so a time can vary twofold between runs where the files and bytes do not. Counted from the file system: a file
is written when it is new or its size or change time moved, and R5.5 names the classes. This repository was measured in a fresh worktree at
main `77a311c967`, then switched in place to `bfdb82acd6`, a day of main later (2,052 files differ). The sibling .NET repository was measured
in a scratch clone under `_fixtures/`, nothing written into it, at two commits of its main five days apart (12 merges, 115 files).

**This repository:**

| Step | Today: a fresh tree per session | A kept tree switched in place |
|---|---|---|
| Tracked files | the checkout: 8,286 files, 99.5 MB, 18.6 s | the switch: 2,034 files written and 44 deleted, 37.3 MB, 7.8 s |
| Dependencies | 17,327 files, 253.7 MB (`npm ci` 93 s; TREEDEPS1's copy writes the same files) | none: no lockfile changed |
| The first build of the two desktop test projects | 730 files, 927 MB (`bin/` 835 MB, `obj/` 93 MB), 92 s | with the web's build: 352 files, 489 MB; .NET 81 s, every project compiled |
| The web's build, its contracts generator included | 159 files, 192 MB, 395 s, most of it the type check under load | 479 s, in the line above |
| The removal | 26,797 files deleted, 18.1 s | none |
| **Per session** | **26,502 files and 1.47 GB written, as many deleted** | **2,386 files and 526 MB written** |
| **Per 100 sessions** | 2.65 million files and 147 GB written, 2.65 million deleted | 0.24 million files and 53 GB written |

A kept tree writes 24,100 fewer files and 0.95 GB less per session (91% of the files, 64% of the bytes) and deletes none: per 100 sessions,
2.4 million files and 95 GB less written, and 2.65 million deletions gone. Of today's bytes, 93% are ignored content: runnable outputs 67%,
dependencies 17%, intermediates 9%; the tracked files are 7%.

**In place, under a room** (R5.3): no checkout, no dependencies, no removal, and builds of what the session changed. With the stamp off
(R5.8), the build after 45 files of the driver and the modules changed wrote 81 files and 297 MB (7 of 12 projects compiled), 212 MB of it
copies of the rebuilt assemblies into each test project's `bin/`.

**The sibling .NET repository** (15 projects, 1,443 tracked files):

| | Today | Kept, switched in place |
|---|---|---|
| Tracked files | the checkout: 1,443 files, 15.9 MB, 3.5 s | the switch: 115 files, 4.1 MB, 0.3 s |
| The build | 821 files, 820 MB, 26 s | 283 files, 144 MB, 22 s; every project compiled (the SDK's commit stamp) |
| The removal | 2,278 files deleted, 3.2 s | none |
| **Per session** | **2,264 files, 835 MB** | **398 files, 148 MB** |

There a kept tree writes 82% fewer bytes, most of them the native runtimes MSBuild copies into `bin/` and skips when they are unchanged.

**A clone of a warm tree, emulated on NTFS** (model C): `git worktree add --no-checkout` took 0.2 s; copying a warm tree's tracked files,
dependencies and intermediates, no runnable output (26,279 files, 640 MB), took 47.5 s eight files at once, against 18.6 s for a checkout.
With the warm tree's index copied into the new entry, git listed the tree, and its first `status` (3.0 s) showed nothing but the experiment's
own edit. **Its build at the new path compiled all 12 projects and wrote 935 MB**: .NET's intermediates name their path, so a clone at
another path saves no build.

**The dependency store's placements on NTFS** (the web's `node_modules`, 17,069 files, 229 MB): `npm ci` 72 s; a copy 16.5 s (5.9 s in
D51's quieter measurement); a hard link per file 194 s, made one by one by Git for Windows' `cp -al`, a link being an entry per file too;
deleting either, 9 to 10 s.

**What a view would hydrate** (model A): the two desktop test projects read the 4,699 tracked files of their folders (55% of 8,566), the
web's build 1,932 more (23%), and a harness's search reads every file.

### R5.3 Rooms: a session works in place in the room it holds

**What a room is today.** Two declarations name parts of a repository. `daoris.json`'s `rooms` lists folders with a brief of their own
(`AGENTS.md`), which `sync` points at (doctrine rule 33); this repository declares none, and the sibling .NET repository measured in R5.2
declares three. `daoris.lanes.json` lists lanes: domains that own paths (D115), which the merge tool reads to print the lanes a branch
touched, refusing nothing. Neither is claimed by anyone today. **Round 5's room is a lane that is claimed**: its `paths` as the lanes file
writes them, plus the build units it owns (`builds`: the project, solution, package or module files whose sources lie in it), plus what its
builds and generators read and write outside its paths (`reads`, `writes`), from which the check forms claim groups (R5.4) *(R6-F4)*. One
declaration, the lanes file, so ownership has one home (D228). A doctrine room is a brief's place, a different job, and a room may name one.

**The claim, and what holds a room** *(R6-F2)*. One session works in place in a claim group at a time (R5.4). The claim is a desktop-store
row, `room_claims` (repository, group, room, line, base, run, generation), taken in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction; `base` is the
checkout's `HEAD` when it was taken, where the line's branch is made. **It names the line, and the line holds it**, two ways:
- by `TidyHolders`' rules, read live by the line's path as round 4 reads a slot's holder (§2.3; `Workplace/TidyHolders.cs:39-51`). A line
  running, waiting, parked or spoken to keeps its room, and so does the newest part of an open quest that will go on;
- by its files, while the room's paths hold work of the line's that its branch does not (the private index's status against the branch tip).

A claim whose run is dead by `HomeLease`'s test is taken over only by that line's own carry-on or resume: the look binds it to the new run
in one guarded update that raises the generation, and no other line takes it. So after a shutdown (driving rule 41) each room goes back to
the line carried on in it, and a second quest to that lane takes the fallback.
- **A group is taken** only where its paths stand at the checkout's `HEAD`: their `status --porcelain`, untracked files included, is
  empty, and no other home holds rooms there (break 8, below). Anything else refuses the take, said, a person's change among it, and the start
  takes the fallback. Working files say nothing of who wrote them, so none is read as the person's or a session's.
- **The release** is one journaled act under the checkout's lock, once neither hold stands. Each of the group's paths whose working file
  differs from `HEAD` is written from `HEAD`, but only where that file equals the line's branch tip or the group's base, so everything
  written over is held by a ref. A path holding anything else refuses the release, said.
- **A line that will not go on with uncommitted work in its room** keeps the room, said on the Projects row, until one of three things:
  a session of that line commits it; the person discards it, the discard's journaled act keeping the files under `refs/daoris/discarded/`
  before it writes the paths back (git rule 40); or, only on the owner's yes to §18 item 3, Daoris keeps them aside as row i's keep does
  and releases the room. Daoris never commits them onto the line's branch (§19).

**The record** *(R6-F1, R6-F2)*. A room's line is recorded with its line's path, `s-<8 hex>`, never made a folder, as a pooled line is
(§3.1). Its claim says where it works, and no record names the checkout. A reader that does not know rooms meets the line as a gone tree.
It never reads the checkout's `HEAD`, the line, as the session's branch, nor the checkout's status as the session's work. An older build
goes on in a fresh tree from the branch, and the room's uncommitted files stay held by its claim, said, never lost. `CheckoutBeside`
never waits for a room's session, and waits as today for a session in the root (rule 12's mode). Row 2's census is round 4's (§3.2) for a
room's line: each reader that opens a record's tree asks `WhereIs(line)`, which answers the checkout and the room. While its group is held,
a room's branch is never followed or brought up to date (`TreeLandSyncs`, `TreeUpdates`, a pull's replays), since a moved branch would
disagree with the room's files at its base. Its next landing merges as a tree's does. The agent's folder is the checkout (or the room's
brief folder), where its conversation is found whichever line worked there before it.

**Working in place.** Its branch `daoris/s-<8 hex>` is made at the group's base without a checkout (`git branch`). No session touches the
checkout's index or `HEAD`. Daoris commits for it, through a private index per line:

1. `GIT_INDEX_FILE=<home>/work/index/<line>` and `git read-tree <branch tip>`;
2. `git add -A -- <the room's paths>`, or the paths the agent names within them, the lanes' globs as git's `:(glob)` and
   `:(exclude,glob)` pathspecs, proved against `git ls-files` *(R6-F5)*;
3. the repository's `pre-commit`, `prepare-commit-msg` and `commit-msg` hooks, run with the private index named (`git hook run`, git
   2.36, §25's floor), a refusal refusing the commit in the hook's words; then `git write-tree` and `git commit-tree <tree> -p <branch tip>`
   with the message, signed where `commit.gpgSign` says, and `post-commit` after *(R6-F5)*;
4. `git update-ref refs/heads/daoris/s-<8 hex> <new> <old>`, a ref act of its own in the journal, keyed by its line, not its checkout. It
   writes no checkout, only its branch, its private index and objects. So it takes neither the checkout's lock (`Workplace/ActRuns.cs:113`)
   nor the checkout's one open act (`Workplace/ActJournal.cs:173`) *(R6-F5)*.

Every path outside the room stays at the branch's own version, so the branch holds the room's changes and nothing else. The agent commits
by Daoris's tool (`commit`, with a message and its paths) and reads its status and diff against its branch by the same tool, scoped to its
room. D164 point 1, Daoris commits nothing, is amended: the commit is the agent's, made through Daoris's door (§18 item 7).
- **The commit's check** *(R6-F3)*: `commit` refuses where the room's files no longer hold the branch's own changes, unless the agent names
  that path as a revert it means. That is a path the branch changed since its base back at the base's content, or a file it added gone. It
  stops the session for the person instead of committing the revert.
- **Refused in place** *(R6-F5)*: a sparse checkout, and a checkout whose index holds a skip-worktree or assume-unchanged entry in a room's
  paths. `read-tree` writes neither bit, so `add -A` would stage a file the person keeps out of their commits, or every absent path as a
  delete.

**The line's move under held rooms** *(R6-F1)*. Every way a line moves in its checkout refuses one that is not clean, or one a session
works in:
- Daoris's merge press merges in the line's integration tree, then fast-forwards the checkout (`Workplace/TreeMerges.cs:139-145`,
  `:157-160`);
- the promotion runs `merge --ff-only` in the checkout and never moves a ref under it (`Workplace/IntegrationPromotes.cs:402-410`);
- the line's pull does the same (`Workplace/LinePulls.cs:57-77`, `Workplace/TreeSync.cs:224-226`);
- this repository's merge tool merges in its main checkout (`tools/merge-branch.mjs:3164-3174`, `:5237`).

Round 5's index move left a working file at the old content where the move changed a path the room's session never wrote, and the next line
in that room committed it as a revert. Round 6 replaces it with one act. **The room-aware fast-forward** is an act of the journal, under the
checkout's lock. It is used for every move of a line that a checkout has out while a group there is held: the promotion's effect, which
Daoris's merge press reaches, and the line's pull. With no group held it is today's `merge --ff-only`, unchanged.
- **Judged under the hold.** `HEAD` names the line at `O`, the move `O..N` is a fast-forward, the held groups are read live, and the paths
  the move changes are split into *held* (in a held group's paths) and *free*. Every changed path's index entry must be `O`'s. The checkout
  must be clean outside the held groups, today's rule read for those paths alone. Otherwise the move is held, said, with nothing written.
- **Its steps:**
  1. the index's snapshot under `refs/daoris/acts/` (git rule 40);
  2. a new index built on a copy, each changed path's entry from `N` and every other entry as it stands;
  3. the free paths' working files written from `N`, a deleted one removed;
  4. the copy put in place under git's own `index.lock`, as the act engine's index step does (`ActSteps`);
  5. one `update-ref refs/heads/<line> N O`;
  6. the repository's `post-merge` hook (`git hook run`), as `merge --ff-only` runs it.

  A crash before step 5 is reconciled by writing the free paths back from `O` and the index from its snapshot; a crash after it, by running
  the hook. The move is tried again at the next look.
- **A held path's working file is never written.** It holds its group's base or its line's work. The room's private index reads its branch,
  which holds there the base's content or the line's own change, so the line's next commit reverts nothing. Its landing takes the line's change by the merge, three-way,
  as a tree's would. The group's lagging paths are written at its release, which hands the next line a room at the line's tip. Round 5's
  read's example is answered by this: a fallback branch that changes a held room's path lands, the room keeps its file, and nothing reverts.
- **It never waits for a room.** This repository's line moved 850 times in the seven days from 2026-11-01 to this round: 111 to 165 a day
  on the five busiest, a median 4.7 minutes apart, 90% within 24 minutes. A move that waited for every room's release would stop the line for
  as long as any session in place ran, and a parked one until the person answered. The read's rule, holding a move that touches another held
  room, holds every later move behind it, since the line moves one commit at a time. Both are rejected (§19).
- **A held group sees its own base.** A session's build then reads its own room's work and its group's other paths at its base, never a
  landed change in half its closure. A person sees those paths as modified while it holds them, said on the Projects row as held at the
  room's base.
- **The landing door** *(R6-F1)*. A room's line is landed by its line, never by a tree path. `TreeMerges`' tree refusal (`:61-65`) admits a
  room's line, its branch read from the record and never from the folder's `HEAD`. Its *uncommitted* refusal (`:95-101`) reads the room's
  private-index status against the branch, and its clean-root refusal (`:139-145`) is the fast-forward's own judgement.
- **This repository's merge tool** is this repository's, not Daoris's (§18 item 9). It merges and gates in the main checkout and refuses one
  that is not clean. This repository's checkout takes no room until two things are true: the tool forms and gates in a tree of its own (it
  forms a candidate in its scope tree already, `tools/merge-branch.mjs:7648`), and it moves `main` through the fast-forward's door, a verb
  of Daoris's.

**The harness** *(R6-F3)*. A harness's own git and writes reach every room. A session's `git checkout -- .`, `restore`, `stash -u`,
`reset --hard` or `clean -fd` puts every room's files back at `HEAD`; a habitual `git commit -a` commits every room's work onto the line.
- **In place only for a harness that takes Daoris's rules and its tree guard**, `adapter.Can.TakesSettings` as `OpinionPosture.Of` reads it
  (`Participants/Domain/OpinionPosture.cs:17`): Claude Code, by either door. Codex takes no rules file (`docs/areas/git-and-remotes.md:159-160`);
  where Daoris needed it held as a reviewer, its copy alone held it (`OpinionPosture.cs:13-14`). A harness a Claude Code rule means nothing
  to is handed nothing (`Loop/Session/SessionRules.cs:39-42`). So
  Codex and every plugin's agent take the fallback for every start, said with the reason, until Daoris can hold them as it holds Claude Code.
- **What that session is handed:**
  - deny rules for every git subcommand that writes a working tree, an index, `HEAD` or a branch, each in every spelling, as `no-push` takes
    push's (`git <sub>`, `git <sub> *`, `git -* <sub>`, `git -* <sub> *`; `Agents/Permissions.cs:181`), with `EnterWorktree` denied. Its
    reading forms (`status`, `diff`, `log`, `show`, `blame`, `grep`, `ls-files`, `stash list`) stay;
  - a hard denial the classifier reads: this checkout is shared, so git writes nothing in it; commit with Daoris's `commit`, read with its
    `status` and `diff`;
  - the tree guard, forced on whatever the person's defaults, as a partner's is (`SessionRules.cs:92`). It is narrowed to the room's paths
    and to what git ignores, and its shell judgement refuses those subcommands by the command's structure;
  - `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` in its environment, as `HelperCapture` hands it (`Participants/Helpers/HelperCapture.cs:12`, `:90`), so the
    harness's own `status` never holds the `index.lock` a fast-forward needs;
  - its instruction: it shares the checkout, its git reads only, it commits as it goes through `commit`, and a builder it sends into the
    harness's own worktree starts from the line without its room's work, so it commits first and names its branch.
- **What protects the work whatever ran**, whether an npm script that runs git, a guard that timed out (`Workplace/TreeGuard.cs:23-25`) or
  the person's own tools:
  - the commit's check above;
  - **the turn's end**. `HEAD` still names the line. Each move of the line since the last read is an act of the journal or touches no held
    path (`git diff --name-only <last> <tip> -- <held paths>`). The held paths' index entries compare by mode, object and stage, never by
    stat data, against those Daoris last wrote. Anything else stops every session whose group it touched, for the person, and Daoris undoes
    nothing;
  - **the keep at each turn's end** (§18 item 7). The room's working files are written through a private index of their own to
    `refs/daoris/rooms/<line>`, a recovery ref and never the branch. What a stray reset, clean or *Discard all* destroys in a room is then at
    most that room's last turn.

**Parallelism** *(R6-F4, R6-F11)*. It keeps one session in place per claim group, every group at once. A repository whose rooms' closures
do not meet runs a session in each. In this repository the groups come to about one beside the records (R5.4), so in place serves one
session at a time there. The dispatch skill's builders are the harness's own worktrees and are unchanged. It loses two sessions in one
group at once, which today's trees allow and then merge with conflicts. It also loses a session's licence to write anywhere: a change in
another room is asked of it by a quest, as a change in another repository is.

**Builds in place** *(R6-F4)*. The agent runs its own builds and gates in its shell, so no lock of Daoris's can hold one (R5.7, model B).
- **Claim groups hold them instead.** Rooms whose closures meet are one group with one session in place, so no two sessions in place build
  one unit, race on its `obj/`, or run each other's `bin/`. With the fast-forward holding the group at its base, the session's build is its
  own room's work over its base.
- **What still crosses** is a repository-wide gate that reads every room (here `npm run quick`, `package.json:16`). The session's instruction
  says so, and says that the landing's gates in the integration tree are its proof.
- **The checkout's build outputs are the checkout's.** They are built in place, warm, and never cleared by Daoris, since they are the
  person's checkout's files and a running program may hold them open. A runner that does not build (`--no-build`, this repository's
  `e2e-host.mjs` and `desktop.mjs run`) may meet an earlier line's or the person's build, so the instruction says to build before running
  one. A kept slot, a tree of Daoris's own, clears its runnable outputs at a take as R5.5 says.
- **Per-session outputs** (for .NET a session's `ArtifactsPath`, for others the toolchain's own output setting) are not row 2's answer
  (§19). Every session's would start cold, as this repository's first build does (730 files and 927 MB, R5.2), which is the churn rooms
  end. Sources would still cross. Under claim groups, outputs per room are not needed either.

**What breaks, named:**
1. **A harness that runs git itself**: *The harness*, above.
2. **Builds that see another room's work** and 3. **two builds over one set of outputs**: *Builds in place*, above. Round 5's lock per
   shared unit is withdrawn *(R6-F4, R6-F11)*.
4. **Lockfiles shared across rooms.** A lockfile belongs to the room of its build unit (here the root's to the tools' room, the web's to the
   web's); a package another room needs is asked by a quest. The dependency folder a lockfile installs is an input of every room whose
   build reads it, so they are one group. In place the folder is the checkout's own: the store (R5.6) serves the fallback alone *(R6-F10)*.
5. **Generated files.** A tracked generated file (`docs/code-map.json`) belongs to its generator's room. An ignored one written from any
   room's tools (`docs/index/`, the contracts' generated types) is written under its writer's own lock, as `docs/index/` already is.
6. **Paths no room owns.** This repository's documents are laneless, written by any change; under rooms they are a room of their own, and
   an area document goes with the room it describes (R5.4).
7. **Hooks.** Nothing checks out at a start, so no `post-checkout` hook runs, and a tool that needs `docs/index/` runs its writer; the
   fast-forward runs `post-merge`.
8. **The person's own work in the checkout** *(R6-F7)*. The checkout shows the sessions' work and the held groups' bases as uncommitted
   changes, each said on the Projects row as a held room's.
   - Daoris's Stage, Unstage and Commit are refused for a held path and allowed for the rest, since a commit of free paths moves the line
     as the fast-forward does. Its Switch and its Stash are refused while any group is held (`Workplace/StageActs.cs:147`, git rule 39 read
     so).
   - The person's own tools are not held: a *Discard all*, `git checkout -- .`, `stash`, `switch`, `reset --hard` or `clean` reaches every
     held room, and a `commit -a` commits their work onto the line. The turn's end catches the second and the keep bounds the first.
   - **Another home** that registers the checkout (a development run from source among them) sees no claim of this home's. So the first
     claim writes a marker in the checkout's common git directory naming the home, and the last release removes it. Every other home refuses
     in-place and in-root starts there.

**What a repository gains.** A repository whose sessions do not overlap in a claim group writes no tree for them: no checkout, no
dependency copy, no first build, no removal, its builds warm in place with the stamp off (R5.8), only what changes written. Here that is
everything R5.2's *today* column counts, for the one session in place. The install's work workspace holds 29 repositories; how many ran two
sessions at once in one group last week is row 2's count, read from the install's own records before any tree is retired (row 5).

### R5.4 The way into rooms

- **On the fallback until rooms are declared.** A repository with no rooms works as R5.7 says. One may declare some rooms and leave the
  rest: a session whose work lies in no declared room takes the fallback.
- **Daoris proposes rooms** from the repository's structure: its folders, its build units (a solution and its projects, packages, modules,
  workspace members), its lanes, and the paths its gates declare. Ask Daoris or the set-up proposes them as a change in that repository's
  lanes file, which the person accepts there; for another repository, it is asked of its owner by a quest. The conventions it reads build
  units by are canon knowledge, a document, never code; with no model available nothing is proposed, said with the tier that ran.
- **The check of a proposed room**, deterministic and stack-agnostic, reading only declared paths and git's tracked files: no two rooms'
  paths overlap; every tracked path is in one room or is said to be in none; every declared build unit's sources lie in one room; every
  lockfile lies in one room; and no generated file (tracked, or declared an output in R5.5) is written from two rooms' sources. A failed check
  names the path and the two rooms.
- **Claim groups, from each room's build closure** *(R6-F4)*. A room's closure is its own paths and its `reads` and `writes`: the units
  it builds and those they reference, the inputs outside their folders (props, central package files, a version file), the sources its
  generators read, the dependency folders its builds read, and the outputs its builds write into another room's paths. The conventions
  propose them and the check proves what git's tracked files can show: a unit's folder in one room, a referenced unit's room in `reads`.
  Rooms whose closures meet are one **claim group**, and a group's paths are the union of its rooms' closures. One session works in place
  per group (R5.3), so two rooms whose builds cross never build at once in one checkout, until model B builds them apart.
- **This repository's rooms**, from its lanes and the check: **web** (web-shell and web-settings joined, since they are one package, one
  lockfile and one build), **driver**, **modules**, **service** (the contracts with it), **cli**, **tools** (the root lockfile with it),
  **records**, and **docs** (the laneless paths, an area document going with its room's code). The lanes stay the dispatch skill's map;
  joining the two web lanes as one room is row 1's.
- **This repository's groups** *(R6-F4)*. Its closures meet almost everywhere:
  - the contracts (the service's) are built by the driver, the modules and the service, and `tools/contracts` compiles them with the
    modules (`tools/contracts/ContractsGen.csproj:19-20`) to generate the web's types;
  - the web's build writes `wwwroot` into the service's paths, which the modules' `DesktopPage` serves;
  - the root `Directory.Build.props` and `canon/canon.json` (laneless) feed every desktop build, and `Directory.Packages.props` (the
    modules') sets every desktop project's package versions;
  - the root lockfile's `node_modules` (the tools') is the CLI's, a workspace of the root package, and the CLI reads `canon/`.

  So web, driver, modules, service, tools, cli and the laneless paths are one group, and the records, which no build reads, are a second.
  In place here is one session at a time beside the steward's records; every other start of this repository takes the fallback's kept
  slots until model B, and none takes a room before its merge tool moves `main` through the fast-forward (R5.3, §18 item 9).

### R5.5 Ignored content by class, and its declaration

*Round 7* *(R7-C8, C9, C10)*: nothing a slot carries is trusted because its path stays put. Each class it carries has a key and a check,
and the declaration acts only as the owner accepted it.

*Round 8* *(R8)*: the classes are the inventory's. Daoris reads them on each machine and acts on each entry only once the person has
accepted it (§28.3, §28.5). The declaration below is one input of that reading (§28.4, source 3), the repository's word to it: each of
its fields is taken into the entry it proposes, and the gates file's acceptance no longer covers it. So read *the accepted declaration*
below as *the accepted entry*, and *with nothing declared* as *with nothing accepted*. Two classes join the table, tool caches and
another repository or tree; a path no source names is *unknown*, handled as the table's scratch. *Ask Daoris proposes a declaration*,
the section's last sentence, is withdrawn: the analysis proposes.

*Round 9* *(R9)*: the classes are the rule file's kinds (§29.2), and what acts on a path is its effective rule, read from four layers,
with no acceptance. Read *the accepted declaration* and *the accepted entry* below as *the effective rule*, and *the declaration* as the
repository's own layer, `daoris.files.json`, which takes the gates file's `trees` in the same chunk (D228) and is read from git's
objects at the line's tip. Its example is §29.2's. Three definitions change. A runnable output is what a runner uses **without its
tool's own check** (Opus round 7's F11), a `dotnet build --no-restore` over another line's `obj/project.assets.json` among it. A
dependency's `paths` are everything its one install makes in the tree, a generated client among them (Codex round 7's 3). The `key`
statement reads *no other file, tracked or ignored, changes what `install` makes* (Opus round 7's F8). The uv example's `.venv` takes
`"caches": ["**/__pycache__/"]` and `uv python find` in its `toolchain`, and since a rule matches only the ignored tops git lists, its
intermediate `**/__pycache__/` never reaches inside the `.venv/` (Opus round 7's F10). *(R11-F8, F9)* Its interpreter is keyed by `uv
python find --system` and that interpreter's `--version`, never the `.venv`'s own, and its install is `uv sync --locked`. *(R11-C1)* A
runnable output is found only by a build tool's output setting or a generation step (§29.4).

| Class | What it is | Between one line and the next | Examples |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Dependencies** | what a package manager installs from a lockfile, by the command the declaration names | kept or placed by its key and its placement record, read at every take (R5.6), never linked; one that names its own path is kept in place, never stored, and installed again when its key or record moves *(R7)* | `node_modules`, `vcpkg_installed`; in place: a `.venv`, a conda environment |
| **Intermediates** | what a build tool checks itself and makes again when stale, valid only at the path it was made | carried only by a tree kept at one path, the checkout under a room or a kept slot, and while the tree's `toolchain` key holds; renewed when it moves *(R7)*; never two trees' at once, never cloned to another path | `obj/`, cargo's `target/`, `.gradle/` |
| **Runnable outputs** | what a runner uses without building first, or what its writer makes again cheaply; a folder holding both is an output unless every runner builds first *(R7-C10)* | never carried: cleared at every take | `bin/`, `dist/`, `wwwroot`, `docs/index/`, a CMake build tree `ctest` runs |
| **Local** | machine-local inputs the gates read | copied in read-only at every open (TREELOCAL1), never stored or cloned | `localInputs` |
| **Scratch** | everything ignored that no class names | never carried: it goes with its session's record (§10) | `_fixtures/`, logs |

**The declaration**, in `daoris.gates.json` beside `localInputs`. *(R7)* It acts only as the owner accepted it: what a take clears, and
every command Daoris runs from it, are read from the gates file's accepted blob, and only where that is the line's tip's declaration, as
the tidy's declared scratch is (`Workplace/TreeScratch.cs:46-51`; D218's note). Never a working file's, never a tree's: what crosses from
one line to the next is the checkout's accepted say, never a branch's. A declaration not accepted, or changed since, carries nothing and
runs nothing, said. Its paths are written as the gates' `paths` are: repository-relative, forward slashes, globs, a folder ending in `/`. A
declared path git tracks is refused, since it is git's. Each dependency names:
- `path`, or a list where one install writes several (yarn's PnP files);
- `install`, the command that installs it, run at the tree's root;
- `key`, the tracked paths or globs whose content keys it with what R5.6 adds: the repository's statement that no other tracked file
  changes what `install` makes. With no `key`, the commit's whole tree keys it;
- `toolchain`, the commands whose output keys it (its runtime and its package manager);
- `env`, the variables its install is given beyond the system's own, each keyed;
- `caches`, the folders inside it that its tools write and check themselves, whose added files never fail its record;
- `bound`, where it names its own path, so that it is never stored (its fill finds most such by itself, R5.6).

The tree's own `toolchain` keys its intermediates. The local class is `localInputs`, unchanged (D216), never declared twice. *(R7)*
`prepare`, and the `safe` list it was judged by, are withdrawn: their one remaining use was a template, gone with the owner's no to a Dev
Drive, and each dependency's `install` fills the store. This repository, as row 3 writes it:

```json
"trees": {
  "dependencies": [
    { "path": "node_modules/", "install": "npm ci",
      "key": ["package-lock.json", "package.json", "src/Daoris.Cli/package.json"], "toolchain": ["node --version", "npm --version"] },
    { "path": "src/Daoris.Web/node_modules/", "install": "npm --prefix src/Daoris.Web ci",
      "key": ["src/Daoris.Web/package-lock.json", "src/Daoris.Web/package.json"], "toolchain": ["node --version", "npm --version"],
      "caches": [".cache/", ".vite/", ".vite-temp/"] }
  ],
  "toolchain": ["dotnet --version"],
  "intermediates": ["src/**/obj/", "tools/**/obj/"],
  "outputs": ["src/Daoris.Contracts/bin/", "src/Daoris.Desktop/*/bin/", "src/Daoris.Service/*/bin/", "src/Daoris.Devkit/*/bin/",
              "tools/*/bin/", "src/Daoris.Service/Daoris.Service.Http/wwwroot/", "src/Daoris.Cli/dist/", "docs/index/",
              "src/Daoris.Web/src/data/generated/", "src/Daoris.Web/src/bridge/generated/"]
}
```

The root's `npm ci` runs its `prepare` script, which writes git's configuration in the common directory and `docs/index/`, a declared
output, and nothing in `node_modules`; so the files that script runs are not in its key.

A Python repository on uv declares its `.venv` a dependency kept in place, since its launchers and editable installs name its path. uv's own
cache outside the tree makes the install cheap in each tree, and a slot keeps it at its key *(R7-C10)*:

```json
"trees": {
  "dependencies": [ { "path": ".venv/", "install": "uv sync --frozen", "key": ["uv.lock", "pyproject.toml", ".python-version"],
                      "toolchain": ["uv --version"], "bound": true } ],
  "intermediates": [".pytest_cache/", "**/__pycache__/"], "outputs": ["dist/", "build/"]
}
```

A Rust repository carries cargo's `target/`, since cargo checks every unit's fingerprint and links a binary before it runs it; one whose
scripts run `target/debug/*.exe` without cargo declares that path an output instead:

```json
"trees": { "toolchain": ["cargo --version"], "intermediates": ["target/"] }
```

*(R7-C10)* A C++ CMake build tree holds the executables `ctest` runs as they stand, so it is an output unless every runner builds first.
Gradle's `.gradle/` and `build/tmp/` are intermediates and its `build/libs/` an output. Go keeps its module and build caches outside the tree
and declares nothing; NuGet keeps its packages outside the tree, and restore checks its own files under `obj/`.

**With nothing declared,** nothing ignored is carried: a session's tree is a fresh checkout and its kept slot is cleared of every ignored
path at a take, the local copies made again. An undeclared repository is never wrong, only not faster. **Ask Daoris proposes a
declaration** for a repository that has none, as it proposes rooms (R5.4): from its `.gitignore` files, its tracked lockfiles and manifests,
its install commands and the sizes of the ignored folders in its checkout, each entry with its reason, the canon's conventions naming each
package manager's key inputs *(R7)*, as a change in that repository.

### R5.6 The dependency store

*Rewritten at round 7* *(R7-C8, C9, C10)*. *Round 8* *(R8)*: every field the key and the fill read (`key`, `install`, `toolchain`,
`env`, `caches`, `bound`) is the accepted dependency entry's (§28.3), proposed from the signatures, the scripts and the declaration; the
rest stands.

*Round 9* *(R9)*: every field is the effective `dependency` rule's (§29.2), and the reads of round 7 change seven things, each in §29.6:
- **the key** adds each manager's effective configuration, printed by a `toolchain` command its signature names (`npm config list
  --json`) and run in the folder and environment of the install, the tree's path written as a token before it is hashed (Codex 1, Opus
  F3); each optional project configuration file, absent keyed as absent (Codex 5); and the key of each dependency before it in rule order
  (Codex 2). It is kept as its hash alone. Every `env` value is hashed, never written to a record, a note or a log, and a name a
  rule of any layer marks `secret` is keyed by its name (Opus F8) *(R10)*;
- **the fill** runs at the files stage, after the record's open and before the agent is handed, never in a take's claim (§29.7; Opus F1,
  Codex 4), one install of a repository at a time (Opus F9). It is recorded `installing` before its folder is cleared, so a cut-off
  install is known as Daoris's own (Opus F4);
- **it seals** only in a tree that held, as it began, nothing ignored but the folders of the dependencies before it whose records hold (a
  fresh tree before its hook and its local copies), and only where it wrote nothing in the tree outside its rule's `paths`. *Nothing new beside the
  folder but declared outputs* is withdrawn (Codex 1, Codex 3). An install it cannot seal is the tree's own, recorded and kept in a slot
  by its key and record as a `bound` one is. A `secret` value found in what it wrote seals nothing, as the tree's path does *(R10)*;
- **after each install** every placement before it is read against its record; one it changed is rewritten as the tree's own, and the
  install seals nothing (Codex 2);
- **git's common folder**, which this repository's `prepare` writes, is listed before and after (its hooks and its local configuration),
  and what changed is said; anything else outside the tree is the rule's own to answer for (Opus F9);
- **a placement** copies by its entry's manifest, and a source missing or of another size evicts the entry and falls to the install; a
  file its signature names is written last (npm's hidden lockfile, `Workplace/TreeDependencies.cs:268-273`) (Opus F11);
- **a placement whose record fails, or a dependency's folder with no record**, is never removed at a take: it is the line's own (Opus F7).
  Daoris removes only a placement whose record holds, and an install it marked and that was cut off.

*Round 11* *(R11)*: both reads of rounds 9 and 10 change six things, each in §31, whose *Cn* and *Fn* this note uses. *(R12)* Round 12
revises four of them in place (§32): the write-back, the companions, the group and the mark. *(R13)* Round 13 revises two again (§33):
the group's earlier keys and the mark.
- **the key** takes every `env` value as a digest keyed by a secret of the home's (HMAC-SHA-256 under a key made once in the home, never
  synced or shown), never the value, a value marked `secret` among them (C4). The mark's one effect is the fill's scan for the value.
  Round 10's *keyed by its name* and the plain hash of an unmarked value are withdrawn;
- **an install runs only in an unworked tree** (§29.7), so round 7's *the tree about to be used, its tracked files at the commit and no
  session working there* holds again, for every install Daoris runs, a fill or not (F1, C3). In a tree an agent has run in nothing is
  installed, removed or written back: a dependency whose key moved, or absent at a miss, is the line's to install, said;
- **an install that changed a tracked file, or wrote a path git does not ignore at `HEAD`**, has the file written back from the commit
  and the path removed, each Daoris's own of a moment ago in an unworked tree, and its folder cleared, said: a fill's or not, so no
  install of Daoris's leaves a change the session would commit as its own. *(R12-F3)* One cut off before its after-read leaves its
  change to the next start in the still-unworked tree, whose first step writes back every such change before any placement (§29.7);
- **its companions** (F2): each path in the tree an unsealed install wrote outside its `paths` that git ignores at `HEAD` and no
  effective rule names, listed in its record with its manifest. They are the dependency's: read with its namespace at every take, the
  judgement and the finish, kept with it and never `unknown`, the placement kept only while they hold and else installed again in an
  unworked tree. *(R12-C)* A path another rule names keeps that rule's kind, a take clearing or moving it as that rule says, and is
  listed in the record as the install's **other write**: the placement is kept only while each stands after the stage's hook (§29.7
  step 4), else it is installed again before `hand` in the unworked tree, so its producer runs. This repository's root stays kept at a
  take: its `prepare` writes `docs/index/` where none stands (`tools/index-hook.mjs:84-88`), and the `post-checkout` hook the stage runs
  after a switch writes it again (`:8-12`). Round 11's *its rule answering for its producer* is withdrawn. git's common folder stays
  listed before and after, and said;
- **a group** (C5): where an install changes an earlier placement, the two, with any already grouped with either, are one group: one
  record over every member's namespace and companions, and one key of every member's rule and key in rule order. A group is kept only
  while every member's rule stands and its whole key and record hold; otherwise, in an unworked tree, each member is removed and the
  members installed again in rule order. It seals nothing and is never placed from the store, *(R12-F4)* nor is a member by its own
  key while a group of the tree's record names it, though its own fill sealed before the group formed: in an unworked tree the group's
  install places it, and in a worked tree its absent folder is the line's, said. *The changed placement's record is
  rewritten as the tree's own* is withdrawn: rewritten, it outlived the rule that changed it, and placed again at its key it lost the
  change. Each key still holds the keys before it, for an install that reads an earlier folder without changing it, *(R13-C)* so a hit
  stands for its folder only beside earlier dependencies that are as their keys say: in a worked tree it is placed only where each
  dependency before it was kept or placed by the stage (§29.7);
- **the `installing` mark** is its start's: the `permissions` are read before it is written, and a failed install takes it and its half
  folder away before `hand`, *(R12-F1)* or before a conversation's *(R13-F1)* or a go-on's step that removes `unworked`, so a mark
  found is a cut-off start's (F5). One install runs at a time per key, and per repository only for an install whose rule's last
  install wrote git's common folder, or that has none yet (F9's nit; Opus round 7's F9).

- **The key** of a dependency at a commit: its declared path; each `key` path's blob id at the commit, a path absent counted as absent
  (with no `key`, the commit's tree id); the `install` command; each `toolchain` command's output, run at the tree's root in the install's
  environment, *(R12-F5)* a read in any tree (floor 4); that environment; the system and the architecture; and the repository.
- **The install's environment is composed by selection**: the system's own names (the path, the system's, the user's and the programs'
  folders, the processor), named in code, and the dependency's `env`, nothing else inherited, every name and value in the key. A gate's
  environment, composed by removal (`Review/Domain/GateEnvironment.cs:66-75`), would pass every variable but Daoris's and git's.
- **An entry per key and repository**, `<home>/work/deps/<sha256>`. A dependency's `key` is its own repository's statement, so no other
  repository shares the entry.
- **Filled only by Daoris's own install**, at a miss, never from a tree a session worked in (F6). It runs in the tree about to be used: a
  fresh tree after its checkout, or a slot just switched and cleared, its tracked files at the commit and no session working there. The
  folder is cleared first, and `install` runs in the open's or the claim's job, outside the start's shared hold, under the machine's
  admission as a heavy step, bounded as a gate is, started as an integration gate's command is (`Review/Integration/DirectGateRuns.cs`).
- **The fill's judgement**, from `status --porcelain -z --untracked-files=all --ignored=matching` read before and after and a walk of the
  folder: no tracked change; nothing new beside the folder but declared outputs; no link leading outside the tree; and no file holding the
  tree's path, in UTF-8 or UTF-16, with either slash, or as JSON escapes it. Where it holds, the folder is copied into the store under a name
  of its own and renamed to its key, create-only, a second sealer discarding its own. The manifest goes with it (each file's size and hash,
  each link's target within the tree), and the read-only mark is set on the store's copy alone. It is copied, never moved: a package
  manager's hard links (pnpm's, into its own store) share one file record, and its mark, with that store.
- **Not sealed** where the judgement fails: the tree keeps its own install, said with the reason. A tracked file the install changed is
  written back from the commit first and the folder cleared, since a session starts at its commit and that change is Daoris's own of a
  moment ago. A dependency that writes the tree's path into its files, or says `bound`, is installed in each tree and kept in place in a
  slot (R5.5).
- **Placed** at an open or a take wherever its key hits: copied eight at once (TREEDEPS1's measure), the read-only mark cleared, its links
  made again inside the tree. **Its placement record** is written last, beside the tree's in the home: the key and the manifest, with each
  copy's size, write and change time. A folder with no record is no placement, and is cleared before anything reads it. **Never a hard
  link or a junction**: D51's rejection stands, a write in place or the package manager's own clean reaching every tree's copy (R5.2; Opus
  round 5's F10).
- **Kept** in a slot while its key and its record hold. At every take its namespace is read against the record, stat first and a hash
  where a stat differs: a file, folder or link missing, changed, or added outside the dependency's `caches` replaces the placement. A
  removal of a placed folder takes its links as links first and never follows one (PRUNELINKS1's rule).
- **Nothing placed**, where the declaration is unaccepted or the install fails: the folder is absent, said, a failure in the install's own
  words, and the session starts as a fresh tree does today.
- **It goes** by KEEPSIZE1's size limit and days since last placed, a `deps` kind on the Disk space card. A placed copy is its tree's, and
  its entry's eviction leaves it.
- **It replaces TREEDEPS1's copy from the checkout** (D228; D51's note), so a person's install never reaches a session on the fallback (F6).
  §18 item 10 asks what a repository that declares no dependency gets. It serves trees alone: a session in place reads the checkout's own
  dependency folders, an input of its group (R5.4) *(R6-F10)*.
- **What no check proves** is that `key` names every tracked input. An input an install reads only where it is present (a patch folder)
  passes every check above unread. It is the declaration's to name, the conventions naming each manager's; a repository that cannot bound
  them declares no `key`, and its entry is shared at one commit only.
- **What it saves is time, not bytes**: a placement writes the files an install writes (R5.2: an install 72 to 93 s, a copy 6 to 17 s). The
  bytes are saved by the slot, which keeps a dependency in place while its key and record hold (R5.7).

### R5.7 The fallback: a repository or a path with no room

Every way below manages ignored content by R5.5's classes and takes its dependencies from the store. The figures are this repository's,
per session, from R5.2; a ReFS figure is Microsoft's documented behaviour, not run here (R5.9).

| Way | Written per session | Time per session | The owner's step | Risks | Daoris's tree code |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Today: a fresh worktree | 26,502 files and 1.47 GB, as many deleted | checkout 19 s, copy 6 to 17 s, cold build 92 s | none | none new | none |
| **1. Kept slots switched in place** (§2 to §12) | about 2,400 files and 0.5 GB, none deleted; less with the stamp off | a switch of 1 to 8 s, a build of what changed | none | a line works in a slot: D189 amended (§4), every folder-reader asks the pool (§3.2); a live line's own files (§18 item 4) | round 4's rows a, b and f under R5.5's classes |
| **C. Copy-on-write clones of a warm template**, on a Dev Drive | the clone an entry per file and no data; the build its compiled output (about 95 MB here), its copies cloned | an entry per file (26,000 here), then a whole build every time, the path being new | a Dev Drive, an administrator's step, with `work/` and the package caches on it | no clone across volumes; a disk image to keep attached; on NTFS a clone is a copy, slower than a checkout | the open made with `--no-checkout`, the template's files and index cloned in; a template per repository warmed by its `prepare` |
| **A. A view (ProjFS), as VFS for Git** | what is read: 78% of this tree by its builds, all of it by a search | a provider in every first read | ProjFS enabled, an administrator's step | a provider down leaves the tree unreadable; stock git reads what it hashes; VFS for Git is in maintenance | a file-system provider Daoris hosts, and a git that asks it |
| **B. Build slots apart from editing** | per build: a switch and an incremental build in a slot no line works in | per build: the switch, then a warm build | none | an agent's builds routed through a tool; a dev server holding a slot; paths in errors mapped back | round 4's slot with no line in it, reset by design as the integration tree is; a build tool on Daoris's MCP |
| A content-addressed build cache | outputs replayed, cloned where the volume clones | no compile of an unchanged unit, at any path | none | per stack (MSBuild's project cache plugin, Gradle's cache, sccache); a commit stamp makes every unit a miss | none: a repository's own build choice |

**What each model asked:**

- **C: a clone that git and Daoris both recognise** (*withdrawn by the owner's no to a Dev Drive*, D214's note of 2026-11-07)
  *(R6-F11a)*. `git worktree add --no-checkout --detach <path> <commit>` writes the entry and no
  file; the template's working files are cloned in (`CopyFile2`, or .NET's `File.Copy`, which on Windows 11 24H2 clones on ReFS), its index
  cloned into the entry's folder, and the line's branch made by `git switch -c`, which writes only what differs from the template's commit.
  R5.2 shows git listing such a tree clean. What breaks: the template, the trees and the store must share one volume; the index's stat data
  is the template's, so the first `status` reads again what changed (3 s here); a template holds no runnable output and no local input; and
  the intermediates come along stale, so the build compiles everything. `git worktree repair` serves a moved tree, not a made one: unused.
- **A: the view.** A read hydrates the file whole, so a session here writes most of a checkout's bytes, later rather than sooner. Stock
  git's `status` reads every file whose stat it cannot trust; VFS for Git needed Microsoft's fork of git, whose virtual-file-system hook
  told git which paths were touched, and Microsoft's successor, Scalar, dropped virtualization for sparse checkout, partial clone and
  fsmonitor, now in upstream git. A session's commits would go to the shared object store as a worktree's do, its branch made as today, and
  its dependencies projected read-only from the store; but a provider must run while any tool reads the tree. Not for repositories of this
  size; open for one far larger than any session reads.
- **B: build slots.** An agent's build or test is asked of Daoris's MCP tool (`build` or `test`, naming a gate or a `safe` command), never a
  shim on the path, which a script or an absolute path steps around and a `--no-build` run defeats. Daoris takes a free slot `b-<n>`,
  switches it to the session's branch (under a room the private-index commits keep it current; on the fallback Daoris first commits the
  session's working files to a private ref), clears its runnable outputs, runs the command and streams its output back as the tool's
  progress and result, a slot's paths shown as the session's. A dev server holds its slot for its life, a claim like round 4's served visit,
  stopped with its session; under a room it runs in place. A build slot holds no work and is reset by design, as the integration tree is
  (`IntegrationReplays.cs:76-78`), so no line-in-slot hazard arises, and it ends rooms' breaks 2 and 3 (R5.3) for good.
- **The content-addressed cache** is a repository's own build choice, made in that repository, and works here only with the stamp off.

**The fallback's choice.** Kept slots switched in place (way 1) are the fallback on every machine: the only way measured that writes
neither the whole tracked tree nor the intermediates again, with no step of the owner's. *(R6-F11a)* With the owner's no to a Dev Drive
(D214's note of 2026-11-07) nothing is cloned: a slot is made by today's open at its folder and renewed by moving its builds aside, the
store's placements are copies, and a plain fresh worktree is made only where every slot is held. Round 5's template, its clone inside a
slot, its Dev Drive steps and §18 item 6's second question are withdrawn with it. A and B stay the later step: A only for a repository far
larger than a session reads, B once rows 2 and 4 show builds colliding between rooms or slots.

### R5.8 The stamp: this repository's own row (row 0)

- **Where.** On the SDK's defaults, `IncludeSourceRevisionInInformationalVersion` is on and so is SourceLink, so `SourceRevisionId`, the
  commit git reports, goes into every assembly's `AssemblyInformationalVersionAttribute` (`0.0.1+<commit>`) and each project's
  `*.sourcelink.json`, both inputs of its compile. `src/Daoris.Desktop/Directory.Build.props` sets `Version` from `canon/canon.json`
  (UPDATEVERSION) and neither switch; `src/Daoris.Service/Directory.Build.props` neither; `src/Daoris.Contracts` and `tools/contracts` have
  no props of their own.
- **The design.** A root `Directory.Build.props`, imported by the desktop's and the service's and found by the projects without one: unless
  `DaorisStamp` is `true`, `EnableSourceControlManagerQueries` off (no read of git at all), `EnableSourceLink` off and `SourceRevisionId`
  `dev`, so a development build carries `<version>+dev`. `tools/desktop-publish.mjs` and `tools/service-publish.mjs` pass
  `-p:DaorisStamp=true`, so a stage and a release carry `<version>+<commit>` as today.
- **Measured** in R5.2's fresh worktree, the change made there and removed with it:

  | Build | SDK defaults | Stamp off |
  |---|---|---|
  | cold | 92 s, 12 projects | 67 s, 12 |
  | nothing changed | 12 s (round 4) | 16 s, none |
  | after a switch writing no file | 51 s, all 12 (round 4's read) | none, by the unchanged case |
  | after 30 minutes of main (45 desktop files) | — | 69 s, 7 |
  | after 7 hours of main (380 files, the contracts among them) | 191 s under load, all 12 | 62 s, 9 |
  | after a day of main | 81 s, all 12 | — |

  The assemblies said `0.0.1+dev`, no source-link file was written and no warning was added. The machine was loaded, so the robust figure
  is the projects compiled, not the seconds. The driver, the largest project, changes in most commits and its dependants follow it, so here
  the time saved is smaller than the count suggests; a repository whose busiest project is a leaf saves more.
- **What reads the commit at run time.** `Loop/SystemWiring.cs:469-472` splits the program's version at `+` into the System page's
  *program* and *build*; `Store/DesktopStore.cs:88` keeps it as the store's writer build (`:324`, `:369`); `Instance/InstanceClient.cs:125`,
  `:226-227` names it in the instance hello; the service's `HostVersion.Stamped` reports it (`Routes/KnowledgeRoutes.cs:44`).
  `InstallRollback.This` and `BuildVersionTests` read the number alone, through `HomeLastBuild.Numbered`, which drops what follows `+`
  (`Install/HomeLastBuild.cs:59`). An installed build keeps its commit, the publish stamping it, so none of them changes there. The stage's
  `build.json` already names `version` and `commit` (`tools/desktop-publish/staging.mjs:45-61`), so an installed reader could take it from
  there, but none needs to. In development they read `dev`, so two development builds of different commits name one build in the hello and
  the store's rows; row 0 checks that neither decides anything on it.
- **Its own row, first**, since it helps every builder and session whatever else round 5 builds: BUILDSTAMP1 (§17.0 row 0).

### R5.9 What was not run here, and the sources

- **ReFS block cloning, the Dev Drive and the copy engine**, from Microsoft's documentation (the Dev Drive pages; ReFS block cloning; the
  `FSCTL_DUPLICATE_EXTENTS_TO_FILE` and `CopyFile2` references): a clone shares clusters until one copy is written, within one volume and on
  cluster-aligned ranges; from Windows 11 24H2 the copy engine (`CopyFile`, `CopyFileEx`, `CopyFile2`) clones on ReFS by itself; .NET's
  `File.Copy` calls `CopyFileEx` on Windows, so it and MSBuild's `Copy` task clone there unchanged. This machine is past 24H2 (build 26300
  against 26100) and has Dev Drive enabled (`fsutil devdrv query`), but no ReFS volume, so nothing cloned here.
- **ProjFS and VFS for Git**, from Microsoft's ProjFS documentation, VFS for Git's maintenance notice and git's Scalar documentation
  (upstream since git 2.38); ProjFS is installed here, disabled, and was not enabled.
- **MSBuild's project cache plugin**, and cargo's, CMake's, uv's and Gradle's path-dependence in R5.5's examples, from their
  documentation; only the .NET case was measured.
- **The hard-link figure** is Git for Windows' `cp -al`, file by file; a native `CreateHardLink` caller would be faster, and stays rejected
  (D51) whatever its speed.
- **The install's 29 repositories** were not read; row 2 counts their overlapping sessions from the install's own records.

## 0. What this rests on

### 0.1 The direction

The owner's direction of 2026-10-25, as the brief §1 carries it: each repository keeps a **pool of trees**. A session takes a free tree and
holds it, locked, while it works; at its start the tree is brought to the line (reset to the line's head, its untracked work cleared, its
ignored dependency and build folders kept); the session works on a branch of its own; at its end the tree goes back to the pool, its work on
that branch ready to land, or with no branch where it was done with no output, *done with output* and *done without output* both ends a
person sees. The pool is a managed number of slots, set by the repository's configuration or Daoris's, at most one per session the repository
may run at once; a slot's tree is made lazily; trees are removed only when that number is lowered or the repository leaves, never by age.
TREEPOOL1's row adds that a go-on or a review that needs the work again has the branch checked out in any free tree (`TASKS.md:312`). In
D193's picture it is the workspace and equipment a staff member is handed (`docs/2026-10-21-company-concept.md` §2, part 14): set up once,
kept, and handed to the next member clean.

**What both proposals share, and this design keeps without argument.** The work's identity is never reused, the equipment is (X's opening;
O §0 item 2). A session's branch is `daoris/s-…`, minted once and never handed to another line. The registered checkout is never a slot. A
slot is a linked worktree of the checkout, made by today's open the first time it is needed. The start window stays `TreeLock` and the
ledger's one session per tree stays the unit of exclusion. The claim on a slot is a desktop-store row by FOUNDATION1 §13's rules, taken in one
`BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction and passed on only from a dead process run by `HomeLease`'s test. git's own lock stays the guard against outside
tools, with a new reason that is neither a session's nor an opening's. No `clean -fdx`, no forced checkout, no `worktree add --force`. A slot
holding work no ref holds is never reset. A parked record holds its tree. Nothing of the install's trees is adopted, converted or removed by
this work. Each amended decision takes a dated note. The pool is off by default until its proofs pass, then on for the repository whose owner
opts in.

**Round 2: round 1's own claims the reads found wrong, checked at `f92e95415`.**
