### j. ⚖ A live line's slot handed on, its own ignored paths set aside and laid back. Only on the owner's yes (§18 item 4) *(C2-5.1, R4)*.

Round 2's row j stays withdrawn *(O2-F6)*. Under round 4 a live line's builds are the slot's and stay, so what this row moves is the line's
own (§11).

- **Changes.** §5.2 step 5 admits, for a line that may come back and that `TidyHolders` no longer holds, ignored paths of its own (its
  scratch, a kind §11 keeps from crossing lines); a take of such a slot moves them aside whole into a set-aside folder of the line's in
  `.tidied`, with the slim's own manifest, never deleted at the take; they are laid back where the line returns, to any slot, at the same tip,
  as a phase of the return's claim, and otherwise go by D195's days or the kind's size limit once the line finishes or does not come back
  *(R4-F12)*; the line's builds stay in the slot for the next line; D195's note.
- **The person sees.** A line that comes back after its tree served other work finds its scratch laid back, said in `tree.returned` or
  `tree.returned-elsewhere`.
- **Proof.** A returnable line's `_fixtures/` and `dist/` set aside, not deleted, when its slot is taken, its tracked work, its commits and the
  slot's `bin/` and `obj/` untouched; laid back at its return into another slot at the same tip, and not where its tip moved, the opening
  naming the folder; the folder going by days or size once the line finishes; a slot holding a repository, a person's link or work no ref holds
  still refused.

**How each input folds in.** FOUNDATION1: every row after mod-x; the tables Workplace's family by §13.3; one port, Settings', answered by
Workplace within D212's three bounds *(O2-F8)*; the standing move read from `moves` *(R4-F9)*; f-i's dead-run test and the act engine's job
and generation reused *(R4-F8)*; row 1d-i's keyed open, built, extended to the pool's opens *(R4-F13)*. D51 and D189 an amendment each (§13,
§4) *(R4)*; D79, D88, D102, D167, D178 and D195 a dated note each, in the row that changes them; D164 only in row i. SWITCHCTX1: its carry,
once accepted, could carry a conversation between two slots' folders (§4). D115: the default
reads `laneCap` once DEV6 builds it. LINESTOGETHER1, ACCEPTCONFLICT1 and REVIEWFLOW1: unchanged; a head merging others does so in its own path.

## 18. What is the owner's

D191 point 3: a change of the product's direction, a spend beyond the development's own runs, a sign-in, a publish, a push or a history
rewrite, and anything that loses the owner's data, which the brief §2 reads as removing a tree, a branch or a folder the install holds today,
or resetting a tree that holds work.

1. **What the count means.** *What it is:* the direction says a repository keeps a number of slots, at most one per session it may run at once,
   and removes trees only when that number is lowered or the repository leaves. This design reads the number as **the trees a repository
   keeps**: when every kept tree is held, a start opens a tree of its own as today, which today's tidy removes once its work is landed, and
   which, where its session made no commit, stays until the person's press, as such a tree does today *(O1-F15)*. *What a yes does:* nobody
   ever waits for a tree, and a busy day still makes a few trees past the count. *What a no does:* the number bounds the
   sessions that run at once in trees, the person's conversations included, and a start past it waits until a tree comes free (Codex's
   reading), which this design does not yet specify, so a no means another round of it *(O2, §3)*. *Where to look:* §8, §5.1 step 5, §1.1
   row 4.
2. **The pool on for the install's repository** (row g). *What it is:* the repository the install works in keeps cap + 1 trees, three today,
   and a session there takes a free one, switched to its new branch in place and checked, instead of opening a fresh one; a tree is handed on
   holding what git tracks at a ref, its npm dependencies and its .NET builds, a finished line's scratch going aside with its record as today
   *(R4)*. *What a yes does:* sets `keptTrees` for that repository, runs row g's installed
   resume (item 5) and starts its day of measurements. *Where to look:* §5, §7, §11, row g, and the proofs of rows a to f. *Round 7*
   *(R7)*: the owner chose on 2026-11-07 that this repository keeps its sessions on kept slots (D214's note). Asked: is that this item's
   yes? If it is, row g follows row 4 with item 5's one resume first. If it gives the direction only, row g waits for this item's own yes.
   *Round 8* *(R8)*: answered yes by D214's note of 2026-11-08. Row g follows rows 6a, 6b, 3 and 4, once the owner has accepted this
   repository's inventory. *Round 9* *(R9)*: it follows rows 6a, 3, 4 and 6b, with nothing to accept: this repository's
   `daoris.files.json` and the found rules act as row 6a writes them.
3. **Daoris keeping a let-go line's uncommitted work aside, and clearing it from the tree** (row i). *What it is:* two parentless commits, one
   of what the line had staged and one of its working files, untracked ones included, on `refs/daoris/kept/…`, made by Daoris off the line's
   branch, where D164 point 1 has Daoris commit
   nothing; compared again right before the tree is reset for another line, and laid back when the line returns. *What a yes does:* trees whose
   sessions left uncommitted work go back to the pool. *What a no does:* such a tree stays its line's, said, until a session commits the work or
   the person discards it, and the pool opens another. *Where to look:* §5.5 and row i's proof. *Round 6* *(R6-F2)*: the same keep releases a
   room whose line will not go on with uncommitted work in it. A yes writes the room's files through its private index to `refs/daoris/kept/…`
   and puts the group's paths back at `HEAD` in one journaled act. A no keeps the room its line's, said, and a start for that group takes the
   fallback (R5.3).
4. **Whether a kept tree may be handed on while its last line may still come back** (row j) *(C2-5.1, R4, R5)*. *Round 5's restatement:*
   it arises only on the fallback's kept slots (R5.7, row 4); a room's session works in place and hands nothing on. Under R5.5's classes a
   slot's dependencies and intermediates are the slot's, its runnable outputs (this repository's `bin/`, `wwwroot`, `dist/`, `docs/index/`
   and the contracts' generated types) are cleared at every take since their writers make them again, and its local copies are made again.
   So what a live line may lose at a take is its **scratch** alone: an ignored path no class names, which this repository's sessions write
   under `_fixtures/`. The options below are unchanged but for that, and the recommendation stays **(a)**: with F5's files declared outputs, a
   line whose only ignored files are outputs no longer holds its slot, so (a) hands on far more slots than round 4's read found. *Round 4's
   text:* with a fixed
   folder, a kept tree's dependencies and build folders are the tree's own and stay valid there, so handing it on moves none of them and deletes
   nothing a build made: the next line builds over them. What is the line's own are the ignored files it made that are not builds the tree
   keeps: its scratch (this repository's sessions write `_fixtures/`) and any output a tool would run in place of the next line's sources (this
   repository's CLI `dist/`). A line *may come back* when it ended but is not finished: a closed quest's line, a conversation not spoken to for
   an hour, unlanded work nobody holds; an open quest's newest part and a conversation spoken to within the hour are held anyway and never asked
   about. *The options:* **(a) No**, the default this design builds: such a tree stays its line's until the line finishes or holds nothing of
   its own; the pool hands on finished lines' trees and new ones, and a busy repository opens trees past the count as today. **(b) Yes, its own
   files moved aside and laid back**: moved whole into a folder of their own under `.tidied`, never deleted at the take, laid back when the
   line comes back at the same commit into whichever tree it takes, and otherwise gone by D195's days or the kind's size limit, whichever comes
   first, once the line finishes; the line also loses its agent's conversation unless it gets the same tree back (§4). **(c) Yes, moved aside
   and not laid back**: as (b), the line coming back without its scratch. **(d) Yes, deleted at the take**: not recommended, since scratch can
   hold what nothing remakes. *Recommendation:* **(a)** for the first build: in this repository most lines finish by landing, so finished lines'
   trees carry the build saving the owner chose, and nothing of a live line's is touched. Choose **(b)** later if row g shows starts going past
   the count because live lines held trees for their own files; (b) over (c), since laying back makes the move aside a delay, not a loss. *What
   a yes to (b) does:* builds row j. *Where to look:* §11, §5.2 step 5, §5.1 choice 4, §4, row j; row g counts how often a tree stayed for this
   reason.
5. **One real resume on the install** (row g's installed proof) *(C2-4.2)*. *What it is:* one Claude Code session and one resume in a pooled
   repository, on one of the owner's accounts, to prove the conversation is found once its tree was taken and brought back. *What a yes does:*
   runs it once, bounded to one session and one resume with nothing forced to a limit, unless an authorization the repository records already
   covers it. *Where to look:* row g.
6. **The Dev Drive, and the fallback's shape** (R5.7) *(R5, R6-F11a)*. *Answered* by the owner's choice of 2026-11-07 (D214's note): no
   Dev Drive, the trees staying on the machine's NTFS volume, and the fallback kept slots switched in place with nothing cloned. Nothing is
   asked. *Where to look:* R5.7.
7. **Sessions working in place in the checkout, Daoris committing for them** (row 2) *(R5, R6)*. *What it is:* D51's rule that a session
   works in a tree of its own, and D164 point 1's that Daoris commits nothing, both amended for a Claude Code session holding a room. It works
   in the registered checkout with git that only reads; its changes are committed onto its own branch by Daoris's door when the agent asks,
   through a private index. At each turn's end Daoris also keeps the room's working files on a recovery ref, `refs/daoris/rooms/<line>`,
   never the branch. The checkout's index and `HEAD` are moved only by the room-aware fast-forward and the person's own acts. *What the
   person gives up in their checkout while a group is held* *(R6-F7)*:
   - Daoris's Stage, Unstage and Commit for a held path, and its Switch and Stash altogether;
   - a clean status: the held paths read as modified, the sessions' work and the held groups' bases;
   - safety from their own tools: a *Discard all*, `git checkout -- .`, `stash`, `switch`, `reset --hard` or `clean` reaches every held room,
     bounded to its last turn by the keep, and a `commit -a` commits the rooms' work onto the line, which stops their sessions.

   *What a yes does:* builds row 2, behind the test's seam until its proofs pass. *What a no does:* rooms stay a declaration and the fallback
   serves every session. *Where to look:* R5.3.
8. **Per-session trees retired where rooms cover the work** (row 5) *(R5, R6)*. *What it is:* once a repository's rooms cover a start's
   work, the planner opens no tree for it. Today's in-root mode (driving rule 12) does the same job, so row 5 makes it one room over every
   path under row 2's rules and removes it in the same chunk (D228) *(R6-F6)*. Until then a repository runs one way or the other, never both,
   and a room's start reads its group's paths alone where `CleanCheckout` read the whole checkout. *What a yes does:* starts in that
   repository work in place. Trees are made only on the fallback. A repository with no trees runs its sessions as that one room: they commit
   onto their own branches and land, where they now commit on the checkout's branch, and a harness Daoris does not hold takes a kept slot.
   *Where to look:* R5.3, R5.4, row 5.
9. **This repository's own rooms** (rows 1 and 2) *(R6-F1, R6-F4)*. *What it is:* in this repository the build closures make one group
   beside the records (R5.4), so in place serves one session at a time. That needs this repository's merge tool, which merges and gates in
   the main checkout today, to form and gate in a tree of its own and move `main` through the room-aware fast-forward (R5.3). While that
   session runs, the main checkout's paths in its group stay at its base and read as modified, and the person's Daoris acts are refused on
   them. *What a yes does:* the merge tool's change is a row of this repository's own, beside row 2, and this repository declares its rooms.
   *What a no does:* this repository's sessions keep the fallback's kept slots, and row 2 is proved on fixture repositories and on whichever
   of the install's repositories have rooms that do not meet. *Where to look:* R5.3 *The line's move under held rooms*, R5.4 *This
   repository's groups*.
10. **A repository that declares no dependency, once TREEDEPS1 goes** (row 3) *(R7)*. *What it is:* today a new tree of an npm repository
    has its `node_modules` copied from the checkout where the lockfiles match (TREEDEPS1). Row 3 puts the store in that copy's place. The
    store serves only the dependencies a repository declares and the owner accepted (R5.5, R5.6), and row 3 removes the copy in the same
    chunk (D228). *What a yes does:* row 3 as written. A repository that declares no dependency gets none placed, and its sessions install
    their own, as before TREEDEPS1, until Ask Daoris's proposal of its declaration is accepted. The files written are the same either way,
    a copy writing what an install writes; the time is not, 72 to 93 s here against 6 to 17 s (R5.2). *What a no does:* TREEDEPS1 stays
    for a repository whose accepted declaration names no dependency, and goes for each once it declares one (D228's condition), a later
    row deleting it once none is left. *Recommendation:* yes: one way, and a person's install never reaches a session (F6). *Where to
    look:* R5.6, §27's row 3. *Round 8* *(R8)*: answered yes by D214's note of 2026-11-08, and item 2's yes there puts row g after rows
    3 and 4, which round 8 keeps, rows 6a and 6b going first. Under round 8, *declares* reads *has accepted on its page*: the analysis
    proposes a repository's dependencies when it is registered, so it gets none placed only until the person accepts them, where round
    7 needed a declaration written and landed in the repository. *Round 9* *(R9)*: *declares* reads *has an effective `dependency` rule*,
    which the analysis writes at registration wherever a signature names one, so a repository waits for nothing.
11. **Local configuration and secrets copied into a session's tree** (rows 3 and 4) *(R8)*. *What it is:* today TREELOCAL1 copies the
    `local/` files a repository's gates file names into every new tree, read-only, with no acceptance (D216). Round 8 reads every
    machine-local file the analysis finds (an environment file, a local settings file, a key, the gates' `localInputs`) as a *local*
    entry, and copies into a tree only those the person accepts on the repository's page: read-only, from the checkout, at every open
    and take, never into the store or another repository's tree. The harness's and the editor's own settings files are never copied,
    since they carry the person's own grants beyond what Daoris composes for a session. An accepted copy is readable by the session's
    agent and its model provider, and the entry says so before it is accepted. *What a yes does:* row 3 copies the accepted local entries
    in TREELOCAL1's place and removes its own reading of `localInputs` in the same chunk (D228). A repository whose local entries are not
    accepted gets no copies; its sensitive scan falls back to its built-in patterns, as a tree without the file does today
    (`started.local-missing`). *What a no does:* TREELOCAL1 keeps copying `localInputs` without acceptance, and only the other local
    files wait for it; its reading stays, with that condition, until row 7. *Recommendation:* yes: one way, and the person sees what each
    tree is handed. *Where to look:* §28.3, §28.5, §28.7, row 3. *Round 9* *(R9)*: answered by D214's note of 2026-11-08: local
    inputs and secrets are copied only by the person's rule, a harness's and an editor's settings never (§29.2's refusals 3 to 5). Row 3
    removes TREELOCAL1's copying in the same chunk; whether its copies today carry over as the person's rules is item 13. *Round 10*
    *(R10)*: by the owner's principle of the same day a local input is copied where any layer's rule says `copy`, the analysis writing
    it for an input a gate or a script reads; a harness's and an editor's settings are on the floor (§30.1, §30.3).
12. **A runnable output kept from one line to the next where the person accepts it** (row 4) *(R8)*. *What it is:* round 7 clears every
    runnable output at a take, since a runner that does not build would run another line's build (§25's F2). In a .NET repository `bin/`
    holds a copy of every reference, so a kept slot writes it whole each session: here 0.24 to 0.84 GB of the 0.36 to 1.12 GB a kept
    slot writes, and in sibling A 1.8 GB (§28.2). The inventory could let the person edit an output entry to *kept*, its cost said first
    (§15's cost line). *What a yes does:* row 4 keeps an output the person accepted as kept, renewing it only at a toolchain change or the
    size bound, and says at each take that its outputs are another line's until the session builds. This repository's `bin/` would stay
    cleared, since its tools run `bin/` without building. *What a no does:* outputs are always cleared at a take, and a kept slot here
    saves nine files in ten and a quarter to three quarters of the bytes. *Recommendation:* no, for the first build: the hazard is a wrong
    result, not a cost, and row b's note measures what a take writes before anyone weighs it again. *Where to look:* §28.2, §28.5, R5.5.
    *Round 9* *(R9)*: answered for the first build by D214's note of 2026-11-08: outputs are cleared at every take, moved aside (§29.3),
    and no rule keeps one. Asked again with row b's measurement.
13. **What TREELOCAL1 copies today, carried over as the person's own rules** (row 3) *(R9)*. *What it is:* under D216 every new tree
    gets read-only copies of the `local/` files its gates file names (`localInputs`, the sensitive patterns file). Under rules a copy acts
    only from the person's own rule (item 11), so row 3, which removes TREELOCAL1's copying, would leave every tree without them until the
    person presses each proposal. *What a yes does:* row 3's upgrade writes, into the person's layer of each registered repository, one
    `copy` rule per file TREELOCAL1 copies there today, said on the repository's page as carried from D216 and removable like any other;
    nothing changes for a person who sets nothing. *What a no does:* each waits for the person's press; until then a tree gets none, and its
    sensitive scan falls back to its built-in patterns, said (`started.local-missing`). *Recommendation:* yes: D216 was the person's yes to
    exactly those files. *Where to look:* §29.2, §29.5 bound 2, row 3. *Round 10* *(R10)*: answered yes by D214's note of 2026-11-08
    on round 9's choices. Since a copy now acts from any layer, the carried rules keep today's copies whatever the found layer says, and
    the person removes one as any rule of theirs.
14. **A repository's own command run by Daoris only once the person lets it** (rows 3 and 6a) *(R9)*. *What it is:* the commands the
    toolchain signatures ship (`npm ci` in a lockfile's folder, `dotnet --version`, `npm config list --json`) run by themselves. A
    repository rule's `install` or `toolchain` command that no signature ships is run by Daoris outside every session's permission rules,
    so it runs only once the person adds its text to the repository's `commands` in their layer, one press on the proposal, as D218 runs
    the integration's commands only from the gates file the owner accepted. *What a yes does:* as written; a repository on the signatures'
    commands, this one among them, waits for nothing. *What a no does:* a landed rule's command runs as it stands at the line's tip, with
    the trust already given to the lifecycle scripts its install runs, and nothing waits; D218's rule and this one then differ.
    *Recommendation:* yes: one way for Daoris to run a repository's command. *Where to look:* §29.2's refusal 6, §29.4. *Round 10*
    *(R10)*: answered otherwise by D214's note of 2026-11-08 on round 9's choices: a command a rule names runs in every tree Daoris
    makes for the repository, each being for work the person drives or a conversation the person opened, unless the person's
    `permissions` deny it or an `ask` holds it for an allow, which lasts until taken back and may be a pattern (§30.4). D218's acceptance
    of the integration's commands and this differ, as the item's *no* said.

Round 2's item 4, a finished line's scratch cleared at a take, is withdrawn: a finished line's scratch now goes with its record at its finish,
which is today's rule under the owner's decision of 2026-10-24 (D195:503-505), and asks nothing new *(O2-F6)*.

**Not asked:** the install's trees and set-aside folders, since nothing here removes or converts them (§16); lowering a count, which is the
person's own act and whose retired trees wait D195's days or the kind's size limit *(R4-F12)*; **a finished line's scratch set aside at its
finish, and its builds kept in the slot**, since today's tidy already sets a finished tree aside whole and deletes it an hour after its record,
under the owner's decision of 2026-10-24 (D195:503-505, :511-519), and the finish removes less than that rule does *(C2-5.1, R4)*; **a slot
renewed at its size bound**, its builds moved aside, never deleted at the take, and gone by KEEPSIZE1's rule for the set-aside kind *(R4)*; a
publish, a push, a history rewrite or a sign-in, none of which any row needs. **Not asked again:** the owner's choice of 2026-11-07, a fixed
slot path, answers the round-3 reader's proposed item 6 *(R4-F1)*.

## 19. Rejected

| Rejected | Why |
|---|---|
| *Round 4:* a slot moved to each line's path (rounds 1 to 3) *(R4-F1)* | no output that names its path survives a move, so no take saves a build; the owner chose a fixed path, `k-<n>` (§1.1 row 1) |
| *Round 4:* the slot's own path in `sessions.tree` (O-14) *(R4-F1)* | a shipped build's TREEGONE1 answers *stands* for any folder git lists (`TreeReturns.cs:57-65`) and would go on in a slot on another line's branch; every record names its line's path instead (§3.1) |
| A second session family in the service for pooled lines (X) | §1.1 row 1; compatible-builds asks a new table only for rows an older build must not reach, and a path that stays its line's needs none |
| A durable lease per occupation (X) | §1.1 row 2 |
| git's lock alone, a lease file, a lease in the service | not a test-and-set across a read, a judgement and a switch; a file needs the store's dead-run test anyway; the service would learn a machine path (D47 §4; foundation §13.7) (O-1) |
| A permanent pool lock reason (X) | §1.1 row 7 |
| `reset --hard` and `clean -fd` in the first build (O) | a reset of a tree that may hold work before the owner's yes; a clean slot needs neither |
| `clean -fdx`; a forced switch; `worktree add --force` or `--ignore-other-worktrees` | loses the kept folders; overwrites what was there; two trees on one branch (D51:157-158) |
| Loose files moved to a kept area (X) | §1.1 row 3 |
| Daoris committing the leftovers on the line's branch | lands what the agent never chose to commit, and turns a no-output end into output (O-6) |
| The count in `daoris.json`, or in the registry | D115:58-59's reason, and a count is a machine's; the registry travels |
| The count in a store table of its own (X) | §1.1 row 5 |
| The cap at opt-in as a stored default (X) | §1.1 row 6 |
| A start waiting for a tree (X) | §1.1 row 4; the owner's question (§18 item 1) |
| New record tables, refs or a route for a finished line (both) | §1.1 row 9 |
| Removal by age | the direction |
| `git worktree remove` of a retiring slot | TREEKEEP1 refuses ignored paths only the tree holds; the set-aside's move is the path the tidy proves (O-9) |
| A branch named for a slot (`daoris/t-2`) | landings, recovery refs and traces would run two lines under one name (O-2) |
| A junction per line leading to a stable slot | `git worktree remove --force` follows a junction (D51:74-77); what agents and the tree guard read through one is unverified (O-4) |
| Copying an agent's conversation files between folders | writes in an agent's home (D189 point 5); SWITCHCTX1's carry is not accepted (O-4) |
| A new part handed the brief whenever the line's slot was taken, its own last slot free or not (both) | §1.1 row 11: its own last slot is switched back to it first, where its conversation is *(R4)* |
| Copying dependencies at every take | 228 MB for nothing (D51:85-88; O-11) |
| A stale `node_modules` kept untouched (X) | §1.1 row 10 |
| Adopting the install's idle trees as slots | §16 |
| A no-output branch gone for good (round 1's misreading of X) *(O1-F13)* | §1.1 row 12, restated: both grow it again; the licence lives in the store |
| *Round 2:* moving a slot to a line's path before switching it (round 1's §5.3) *(C1-2.1, O1-F2)* | the line's path showed another line's branch for the length of a relink, which TREEFOLLOW1 records and an older resume goes on in; parked, switched and checked first, placed last |
| *Round 2:* a slot's destination keyed by nothing but the slot (round 1's claim key) *(O1-F1)* | two returns of one line, or a take racing a return, both move into one path and the second nests |
| *Round 2:* OPENCUT1's lock through a take or a return (round 1's §2.4) *(O1-F6, C1-1.1)* | any build's strays read a killed return as a cut-off open and delete the returning line's branch; and a take killed between move and switch is no opening to them at all |
| *Round 2:* opening a record without reading the slot after its switch (round 1's §5.3) *(C1-2.4, O1-F4)* | a write after the judgement rides the switch into the new line, and a path ignored only on the old branch is committed by the new agent's `add -A` |
| *Round 2:* retiring a stuck slot *whatever it holds* (round 1's §5.4) *(C1-2.4)* | a partial switch can leave work no ref holds; a stuck slot goes only by today's press, which refuses that and asks again |
| *Round 2:* carrying every listed folder across a move (round 1's §11) *(O1-F3, C1-1.4)* | `.venv`, NuGet's restore files and the build caches read whatever stands at their old path, another line's tree within the hour |
| *Round 2:* the keep made from `HEAD` and the working files alone (round 1's §5.5) *(C1-2.6)* | a path staged at one version and edited to another came back with the second only |
| *Round 2:* a takeover by phase alone (round 1's §2.3) *(C1-2.2, O1-F5)* | a git child may outlive its run, and a rename may land before git writes its registration: the disk is read first |
| *Round 2:* a terminal's hold known to the shell alone (round 1's §7) *(C1-2.5)* | a `--share` loop or a verb would take a slot a terminal stands in |
| *Round 2:* the count's doors at row d (round 1's §17) *(C1-4.1)* | a person could switch a pool on before lines come back through it or slots retire safely |
| *Round 2:* the finished record's inputs left to the store with no table (round 1's §10) *(C1-2.3, O1-F7)* | a line whose slot was taken before it became removable had no tree left to read, and a retried post had no fixed identity |
| *Round 3:* carrying `bin`, `dist`, `build`, `out`, `coverage`, `wwwroot` or `__pycache__` across a move (round 2's §11) *(O2-F1, C2-1.2)* | a kept `dist/cli.js` runs in place of the line's sources; a CMake tree, a test assembly's content root and `out/build` name their old path |
| *Round 3:* dropping `.venv`, `obj`, `target`, `.angular`, `.next` or `.gradle` at a take as already allowed (round 2's §11) *(C2-5.1)* | for a line that may come back, a deletion no decision of the owner's allows; put to the owner (§18 item 4) |
| *Round 3:* carrying a finished line's scratch into the next line's tree (round 2's §5.2 step 5) *(O2-F6)* | it hands one line's notes and settings to another, contradicts rule 4, and grows without bound; it goes with the record by today's rule |
| *Round 3:* `line_records`, a second home for a finished line's record (round 2's §10) *(C2-2.4)* | KEEPSIZE1b's manifest already is that snapshot, written before anything moves or goes, with its resume rule and its hour || *Round 3:* a placed slot whose links are made again after it is placed (round 2's §5.3) *(C2-2.1)* | a crash between leaves a line's path whose links lead into another path, which an older build resumes through |
| *Round 3:* a link carried for where it lies (round 2's §5.2 step 3) *(C2-2.5)* | location is no authorship: a person's junction inside `node_modules` would be retargeted |
| *Round 3:* an open known by its record's liveness (round 2's §2.3) *(C2-2.2)* | an open whose answer was lost and whose record the orphan sweep ended reads as never opened |
| *Round 3:* a dead run's git children taken as ended by the run's death, or by git's lock files alone (round 2's §2.3) *(C2-2.3)* | a git process may outlive its run and write after the takeover read the disk; the job is opened and emptied first, and the pool is offered only where it can be |
| *Round 3:* a served build seen only by its own shell (round 2's §7) *(C2-3.2)* | a headless loop would move the tree another shell serves from |
| *Round 3:* a visit that holds back every claim (round 2's §2.3) *(O2-F7)* | a person's terminal would stop its own line going on in its own tree |
| *Round 3:* the pool's git steps running repository hooks at the parking name (round 2's §5.3) *(O2-F9)* | an install hook writes output tied to the park, so every take in that repository would stick its slot |
| *Round 4:* the parking name, `git worktree move`, the relink and the exclusive hold at a take (round 3's §5.3) *(R4)* | a fixed folder needs none of them; the take runs in the start's shared hold, as an open does |
| *Round 4:* hooks off for good (round 3's §12) *(R4-F14)* | at the folder the line works in a hook's output is the line's; it runs after the check, so it is never read as a stray write |
| *Round 4:* `update-ref -d` for a take's empty branch (round 3's §2.3) *(R4-F6)* | a second way to delete a branch; the journal's `ref.delete` act keeps the commit check and the Undo |
| *Round 4:* a claim keyed by run alone (round 3's §2.3) *(R4-F8)* | two lookers reading one claim dead would both act; a generation fences them, as every other claimed family does |
| *Round 4:* one kept-tree type for the slots and the integration tree now *(R4-F11)* | one holds a line's work and is never reset, the other is reset by design; left open (§20) |
| *Round 5:* `SlotKeeps`, a list of stack folders in Daoris's code (round 4's §11) | Daoris is generic: a repository declares its classes (R5.5), and an undeclared path is scratch |
| *Round 5:* `bin/`, `wwwroot` and the generated files carried in a kept slot (round 4's §11) *(R4-F2, R4-F5)* | each is run, or read, without a build: runnable outputs, cleared at every take |
| *Round 5:* a clone of a warm template per session as the fallback's default | it saves no build, the path being new (R5.2), and saves bytes only on a Dev Drive; it serves inside the kept slot instead (R5.7), and stays the owner's alternative (§18 item 6) |
| *Round 5:* hard links or junctions from the dependency store | a write in place, or a package manager's own clean, reaches every tree's copy (D51); a link is still an entry per file (R5.2) |
| *Round 5:* the dependency store filled from a tree an agent worked in, or from the checkout | an earlier line's or a person's install is trusted by a file's time (R4-F6); the store fills only right after a prepare step |
| *Round 5:* a view (ProjFS) per session now (model A) | builds and searches hydrate most of a repository this size (R5.2); stock git cannot work in a view; a provider must run for every read |
| *Round 5:* a command shim routing an agent's builds to a build slot (model B) | a script or an absolute path steps around it, and a `--no-build` run defeats it; a build is asked of Daoris's tool |
| *Round 5:* sessions in one checkout committing with the shared index | each would commit the others' staged work onto the checkout's `HEAD`; a private index per line instead (R5.3) |
| *Round 5:* two web rooms in this repository | one package, one lockfile and one build: the room check refuses a build unit split across rooms (R5.4) |
| *Round 6:* the landing's index move alone (`git reset -q <new tip> -- <landed paths>`, round 5's R5.3) *(R6-F1)* | a path the move changes that the room's session never wrote keeps its old working file, and the next line in that room commits it as a revert; the room-aware fast-forward writes the free paths and holds a group at its base until its release |
| *Round 6:* a line's move waiting for every room's release *(R6-F1)* | this repository's line moved 850 times in seven days, a median 4.7 minutes apart: it would stop for as long as any session in place ran |
| *Round 6:* a move held whenever it touches another held room's path (round 5's read's F1) | the line moves one commit at a time, so one held move holds every later one; a held path's index alone moves, and nothing reverts (R5.3) |
| *Round 6:* a dead run's room claim taken over by any line (round 5's R5.3) *(R6-F2)* | a room's files are its line's work: after a restart another line's first commit would stage it onto its own branch; only the line's carry-on takes it over |
| *Round 6:* a room released while its files hold what no ref does, or its leftovers committed onto its branch *(R6-F2)* | the first loses the line's work; the second lands what the agent never chose to commit (O-6); the room stays its line's, or the keep takes them on the owner's yes (§18 item 3) |
| *Round 6:* in place for a harness Daoris cannot hold (Codex) *(R6-F3)* | it takes no rules file, and its own git reaches every room's work; it takes the fallback |
| *Round 6:* the harness's git routed through an environment `GIT_DIR` over the shared working tree *(R6-F3)* | it reaches every git the session's tools start, a test's fixture repositories among them, whose `init` or `commit` would write the session's own git directory |
| *Round 6:* one build lock per shared unit, Daoris's own (round 5's R5.3 break 3) *(R6-F4, R6-F11)* | the agent builds in its own shell, so the lock has nothing to take; claim groups keep two sessions in place from building one unit |
| *Round 6:* per-session build outputs (a session's `ArtifactsPath`, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and their kin) *(R6-F4)* | every session's first build is cold, the churn rooms end (R5.2), and sources still cross rooms |
| *Round 6:* runnable outputs cleared in the checkout at a room's take *(R6-F4)* | they are the person's checkout's files, which a running program may hold open; a session builds before it runs one, and a kept slot, Daoris's own, clears them |
| *Round 6:* a room's branch followed or brought up to date while its group is held *(R6-F1)* | the branch would disagree with the room's files at its base, and the next commit would revert what the line brought |
| *Round 6:* in-root sessions and rooms in one repository at once *(R6-F6)* | two ways to work in the checkout with opposite rules for the agent's git (D228); row 5 folds the in-root mode into one room |
| *Round 7:* a dependency keyed by its lockfile and toolchain alone (round 5's R5.6) *(R7-C8)* | a lifecycle script, the manager's configuration or an environment value changes the install at one lockfile, and the old entry's manifest verifies the wrong install |
| *Round 7:* an install run in a gate's environment (round 5's read's F10) *(R7-C8)* | composed by removal, it passes every variable but Daoris's and git's, none of them keyed; the install's is composed by selection and keyed |
| *Round 7:* a fill from only the key's files, in a folder of the store's own *(R7-C8)* | an install's own scripts need the repository (this one's `prepare` runs git and the index's writer), and an input read only where it is present passes it unread: it proves less than it costs |
| *Round 7:* entries shared across repositories at one lockfile (round 5's R5.6) | a key's completeness is its own repository's statement |
| *Round 7:* files added beside a placement tolerated (round 5's read's F10) *(R7-C9)* | an undeclared package installed at an unchanged lockfile would reach the next line; only a declared cache's additions pass |
| *Round 7:* a `.venv` carried as an intermediate for its fixed path (round 5's R5.5) *(R7-C10)* | nothing checks it against its lockfile when a test runs it; it is a dependency kept in place by its key and record |
| *Round 7:* `prepare`, held by a `safe` list in the same gates file (round 5's R5.5) | one commit changes both, no repository declares the list, and its use was a template the owner's no to a Dev Drive removed; each dependency's `install` fills the store, run from the accepted declaration |
| *Round 7:* the classes acted on from the checkout's working file (round 5's R5.5) | what a take clears is a list of files deleted, read from the accepted declaration as the tidy's scratch is (D218's note) |
| *Round 8:* the repository's declaration as what acts, the inventory as its cache (round 7's R5.5) *(R8)* | a repository that ships none (neither sibling does, §28.2) gets nothing handled, and what lies on disk is the machine's; the declaration is one input (§28.4) |
| *Round 8:* the inventory kept in the repository, tracked or ignored *(R8)* | it would travel or be read from a branch, and its sizes, toolchain and private names are the machine's (D47 §4); it lives in the desktop store |
| *Round 8:* two acceptances, the gates file's for `trees` and the inventory's *(R8)* | two ways to say yes to one handling (D228); the inventory's alone |
| *Round 8:* the toolchain's signatures as code, a case per stack *(R8)* | R5.1: no code names a stack's folders; the signatures are data one engine reads |
| *Round 8:* a model reading the repository to propose its classes *(R8)* | not cheap and not deterministic, its tier varying by machine; Ask Daoris may explain an entry |
| *Round 8:* an entry not accepted acting as its proposed class *(R8)* | nothing acts on what the person has not accepted; it is handled as unknown, today's handling |
| *Round 8:* the harness's or the editor's settings copied from the checkout into a tree *(R8)* | they carry the person's grants past what Daoris composes for a session (`Loop/Session/SessionRules.cs`) |
| *Round 8:* a walk of every ignored folder at each reading *(R8)* | 151 s here over 142,763 files (§28.2); git lists the tops, and walks are bounded |
| *Round 8:* last-access times as the evidence of a run without building *(R8)* | not reliable on NTFS; the session job's processes and the scripts' text instead |
| *Round 8:* the names inside a local, scratch or unknown entry kept in the inventory *(R8)* | a private name (here a folder named for a sibling repository) would be written into the store and shown |
| *Round 9:* round 8's inventory, each entry acting once accepted (§28.3) *(R9)* | the owner's choice of 2026-11-08: every finding would wait for a press; rules act, and only what §29.4 names waits for the person's own rule |
| *Round 9:* the repository's rules in the gates file (`trees`) or in `daoris.json` *(R9)* | the gates file is accepted whole for its commands, so every gate's edit would hold the rules back (Opus round 7's F5); `daoris.json` is the doctrine tool's offline contract (D115:58-59's reason for the lanes file); a file beside them keeps one format in both layers, its own blob and history |
| *Round 9:* the analysis's rules above the repository's *(R9)* | a guess would override what the repository states, a key's completeness or a path-bound install among it |
| *Round 9:* the repository's layer read from the tree's commit or the checkout's working file *(R9)* | a branch's change would act on the next line, and on a command Daoris runs outside every session's rules; it is read at the line's tip |
| *Round 9:* the take's install outside the start's hold, before the record opens (round 7's `installed` phase) *(R9)* | a pull's replay reads the slot as free and rebases it under the install (Opus round 7's F1, Codex round 7's 4); it runs in the files stage, under the record |
| *Round 9:* a reservation from `tree_claims` read by every removal and replay judgement (Codex round 7's 4, its first way) *(R9)* | every reader of an in-use set would learn the claims; with the install under the record, no take runs outside the hold before its record |
| *Round 9:* the package managers' configuration replaced by Daoris's own (Codex round 7's 1, its first way) *(R9)* | it would install otherwise than the person's own install does, a private registry's sign-in in the user's file among it; the effective configuration is keyed instead |
| *Round 9:* a fill sealed beside declared outputs, or beside a slot's kept intermediates and caches (round 7's R5.6) *(R9)* | a hit does not make the outputs again (Codex round 7's 3), and an install may read another line's state no key holds (Codex round 7's 1) |
| *Round 9:* a placement whose record fails replaced by removal at a take (round 7's R5.6) *(R9)* | it holds the line's own writes, a patch not yet written or a `pip install` among them (Opus round 7's F7) |
| *Round 9:* outputs deleted at a take (round 7's §5.3) *(R9)* | a scratch folder the analysis took for an output would be lost; moved aside, a wrong kind costs a build |
| *Round 9:* tool caches shared through the store (round 8's §28.5) *(R9)* | its fill would run a repository's script no signature ships; a cache is kept per slot for the first build |
| *Round 9:* two installs of one repository at once *(R9)* | this repository's `prepare` writes git's common configuration, and the second fails on its lock (Opus round 7's F9); a second at one key also installs for nothing |
| *Round 10:* round 9's person-only fields, its refusals 4 to 6, the analysis's *never* list and its proposals *(R10)* | the owner's principle of 2026-11-08: they slowed development to guard what the person's own rules and `permissions` can (§30.2) |
| *Round 10:* `discard`, scratch deleted at its line's finish *(R10)* | it deletes what a session made, which the floor sets aside; the set-aside goes an hour after the record (D195) |
| *Round 10:* the rule file's restrictions in `permissions.json` *(R10)* | it is handed to the harness whole, so an allow given there for Daoris's own act would widen the agent too; and the harness reads an `ask` before an `allow`, so no allow could answer one |
| *Round 10:* a harness's or an editor's settings copied where a rule or an allow says so *(R10)* | it would set a session's permissions past the person's, unseen (§30.3) |
| *Round 10:* every passed `env` value scanned for in what a fill wrote (round 9's F8 answer) *(R10)* | an unmarked value is keyed by its hash, so a hit holds the same value; the scan cost an install in each tree for more than F8 asked |
| *Round 11:* an install in a tree an agent has run in, rolled back to its pre-install working and index states where it fails (Codex's first way for its R11-C3) *(R11)* | a command reaches what no rollback lists, a folder it empties or a file outside its paths, so the floor would rest on the command; nothing is installed there, and the line installs its own as before row 3 |
| *Round 11:* a name marked `secret` keyed by its name, and an unmarked value by its plain hash (round 10's R5.6) *(R11)* | a changed secret keeps its key, so a hit places the old variant (R11-C4); one digest keyed by the home keys every value and lets none be guessed |
| *Round 11:* a script that names an ignored path read as its producer (round 8's source 5) *(R11)* | naming shows a reader, not a recreation, so unique files became an output a take clears (R11-C1); a build tool's setting or a generation step is the evidence |
| *Round 11:* a placement another install changed rewritten as the tree's own (round 9's answer to Codex round 7's 2) *(R11)* | it outlives the rule that changed it, and placed again at its key loses the change (R11-C5); the two are one group |
| *Round 11:* one install per repository at a time (round 9's answer to Opus round 7's F9) *(R11)* | it holds a sealable fill behind an unsealable one (R11-F9); a lock per key, and the repository's for an install that writes git's common folder |
| *Round 11:* the person's `files.json` that does not read read as empty, beside the found layer in the same file (round 9's §29.2) *(R11)* | its defaults restrict nothing, so a stray comma drops every restriction the person set, and the analysis's next write over it loses their rules (R11-F7) |

## 20. What stays unmeasured, and what this design's checks do not cover

- **Round 11's** *(R11)*: how often a resume or a carry-on finds a dependency left as its line's, since no install runs in a tree an
  agent has run in (row g counts it); the disk a live line's set-asides hold until it finishes, beside the outputs moved aside at takes
  (row b's note); how often an install writes companions or forms a group (none here: the root writes `docs/index/`, an output, and git's
  common folder); what a bare-repository candidate costs the walk; and the package managers' install-script marks, from their
  documentation.
- **Round 9's** *(R9)*: the files stage's time on a start's path, which row g splits out; whether `npm config list --json`, and each
  manager's like command, prints the same twice at one configuration (row 3 runs it twice and compares), and what it costs at each take;
  how often a fill can seal here (the root never, since its `prepare` writes `docs/index/`; the web's at each key move); what an install
  writes outside the tree beyond git's common folder, which nothing sees; the disk the outputs moved aside at takes hold under KEEPSIZE1's
  limit; and how often a person is asked for a `copy` or a command, *(R10)* now only where a restriction they added holds one.
- **Round 10's** *(R10)*: nothing new is measured. How often an added `ask` holds an act, and how often a `files` supervisor's grants
  answer it, wait for the supervisor kinds' round (D233's note of 2026-11-08); what a harness reads from a copied settings file is from
  Claude Code's settings documentation, not run.
- **Round 8's** *(R8)*: a reading's time and git starts on a real checkout, the analysis not being built (§28.2's walk is the
  measurement's, not the analysis's); the walk's bound; how much sampling the session job's processes misses of a run without building;
  the siblings' tracked and untracked files, which their matcher cannot split; a kept slot's per-session bytes under round 7's classes,
  recombined here and measured by row b; and how often a reading changes an accepted entry's revision, so how often the person is asked
  again.
- **Round 7's**: what a take's namespace read costs over a `node_modules` of 17,000 files, and a fill's path scan over its bytes (rows 3
  and 4 measure them); which spellings of a tree's path the scan misses (a short 8.3 name, another case), so which path-bound install it
  would store, which `bound` then declares; whether `dotnet --version` moves with every SDK change that leaves `obj/` stale; and an input an
  install reads only where it is present, which nothing here proves (R5.6). How often each key moves was counted from main's history, not
  timed: in the two weeks to this round no lockfile changed, the root `package.json` in 26 commits, so the root's small folder is installed
  again about twice a day and the web's about once a week.
- **Round 6's**: the room-aware fast-forward's time for a large move on Windows; whether the harness's own `status` at a start honours
  `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS`; that `commit-tree` signs and `git hook run` runs as `commit` would, from git's documentation; how long parked
  lines hold a group, which row 2's week counts; and whether this repository's merge tool moving `main` through Daoris's verb keeps its
  gate tree's speed (§18 item 9). The line's moves (R5.3) were counted from main's history, not timed.
- **Round 5's**: R5.9 lists what rests on documentation alone (ReFS clones, ProjFS, the other stacks' path-dependence) and what the
  install's records answer (overlapping sessions per room); R5.2's figures were taken once, under load, the files and bytes firmer than the
  seconds.
- **What the pool saves** *(R4-F1)*: measured once here, in a fresh worktree on a machine running other sessions (§11); row b measures a warm
  build against a cold one in one slot over a line's own day of commits and proves them equal, its note read before row d; row g measures it
  from a start's admission on the install. If the saving is too small to carry the costs of §1.1 row 1, a later round says so with that number.
- **Whether an incremental .NET build after a switch equals a clean one at the same path** byte for byte, which deterministic compilation
  leads one to expect and MSBuild's incremental clean supports: row b proves it before any kind joins `SlotKeeps` but those §11 names.
- **The size bound's default** (4 GB a slot outside `node_modules`), set from one measurement of this repository: row b and row g.
- **How often a line comes back in another slot** and starts from the brief, and **how often a live line's own files keep its tree** until the
  owner answers §18 item 4: row g.
- **Whether a job object stays open by its name while a process in it runs**, which the takeover's proof of quiet rests on: read from the
  platform's documentation, not run; row b kills a claim's process mid-git and proves the job empty before the takeover reads the disk.
- **What a refused switch leaves of the tracked files on Windows**: row b.
- **Off Windows**: the pool is not offered, since the claim's job holds nothing there (`ProcessJob.cs:15-18`) *(C2-2.3)*.
- **Whether Claude Code reads anything of a directory but its path when it resumes**, now in the same slot after another line worked there:
  row g's installed proof (§18 item 5).
- **How often every kept tree is held** so a start goes past the count: row g.
- **An agent other than Claude Code and Codex that keys a conversation by path**: none is read here; a plugin's door is read by its provider's
  contract (D213), which this design does not change.
- **Whether one kept-tree type should serve the slots and the integration tree** *(R4-F11)*: open; this design keeps two makings.
- **A build without the pool beside a pool-aware one on one home**, racing a take: said (§14), not measured; row d's pinned-build rehearsal
  runs it.
- **What this round's checks do not cover.** It is a document: the documentation checks prove its routing and shape, not its claims about the
  code. Round 4's claims were read at `75a4b694be` for every line it writes and every line §24 names (*Read at*); the rest stand as round 3 read
  them at `3dd134cf3`, round 2 at `f92e95415` and round 1 at `f8fbb1bb7` (the reads file, *Moved from the design at round 4*), through
  *Read at*'s map. git's own behaviour (`switch --no-overwrite-ignore`, `-c core.hooksPath`, `git hook run`, `write-tree` from the real index,
  `read-tree` into a private index), npm's workspace links and MSBuild's incremental clean are taken from their documentation, not run. The
  builds of §11 were run once; nothing else was. Both makers' reads of round 4 check it next.

The independent Opus 5.5 reader read round 5 at `353a67a334`, verbatim in `docs/2026-10-26-tree-pool-reads.md` (*Opus (independent),

and toolchain. Where row 2 cannot hold a case, the start that would meet it takes the fallback's kept slot. Every line the table cites was
read at main `5643df4196`.

| Finding | Verdict | Where it went, and the row that proves it |
|---|---|---|
| F1 (blocking) A room's branch cannot land, and round 5's way to land it leaves files a later line commits as a revert | **Holds. Taken, by a rule of round 6's own** | Every way a line moves in its checkout refuses one that is not clean or one a session works in: the merge press (`TreeMerges.cs:61-65`, `:139-145`, `:157-160`), the promotion (`IntegrationPromotes.cs:402-410`, `CheckoutBeside.cs:37`), the pull (`LinePulls.cs:57-77`, `TreeSync.cs:224-226`) and this repository's merge tool (`tools/merge-branch.mjs:3164-3174`, `:5237`). The index-only move leaves stale files, as the read shows. **The room-aware fast-forward** (R5.3) is one journaled act for the promotion and the pull. It writes the free paths in index and working tree and the held groups' paths in the index alone. A held group keeps its base until its release, which hands the next line a room at the tip, so its next commit reverts nothing and its landing merges. With no group held it is today's `merge --ff-only`. **It never waits for a room**: this repository's line moved 850 times in seven days, a median 4.7 minutes apart, so a move that waited for every release would stop it. The read's per-path hold would hold every later move behind one (§19). `CheckoutBeside` is unchanged, since a room's record names its line's path. `TreeMerges`' three refusals are read for a room's line, and a room's branch is never followed while its group is held. This repository's merge tool takes no room until it moves `main` through the act (§18 item 9). Row 2 |
| F2 (blocking) A room's files are its line's work, and the claim hands them to another line | **Holds. Taken as the read proposes, the release made lossless** | A shutdown kills and carries on every session (`docs/areas/driving.md`, rules 36 and 41), and a slot's holder is read live (`TidyHolders.cs:39-51`), so a run's claim cannot stand for a line's files. The claim names the line and its base, and the line holds it two ways: by `TidyHolders` read live, and by its files. A dead run's claim is taken over only by that line's carry-on or resume. A group is taken only where its paths stand at `HEAD`, so "changes no session made" is no longer read. The release is a journaled act writing only what a ref holds. A line that will not go on with uncommitted work keeps its room until it is committed, discarded by the person (kept under `refs/daoris/discarded/`) or, on §18 item 3's yes, kept aside. Daoris never commits it onto the branch. The record names the line's path, so an unaware reader meets a gone tree, never the checkout's line (R5.3 *The claim*, *The record*). Row 2 |
| F3 (blocking) A harness's own git reaches every room: Claude Code held by rules and a hook that fail open, Codex by nothing | **Holds. Taken; Codex and every harness Daoris cannot hold take the fallback** | Codex takes no rules file (`git-and-remotes.md:159-160`). A harness with no rule language is handed nothing (`SessionRules.cs:39-42`). The guard fails open (`TreeGuard.cs:23-25`), and git's area rests no act on the harness's rules alone (`:293-294`). So only an adapter that takes Daoris's settings works in place (`OpinionPosture.cs:17`), Claude Code by either door. It is handed git's writing forms denied in every spelling as `no-push`'s are (`Agents/Permissions.cs:181`), `EnterWorktree` denied, the classifier's sentence, the guard forced on and narrowed to the room (`SessionRules.cs:92`), and `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` (`HelperCapture.cs:12`, `:90`). Whatever ran, three things protect the work: the commit's check refuses a reverted room; the turn's end reads `HEAD`, the line's moves and the held index entries by mode, object and stage; the keep at each turn's end puts the room's files on `refs/daoris/rooms/<line>`, bounding a stray reset to one turn (§18 item 7). A `GIT_DIR` route is rejected for the read's reason (§19). Row 2 |
| F4 (blocking) A build or a gate in one room reads and writes another room's work, and no lock can hold it | **Holds. Taken: claim groups by build closure; per-session outputs rejected** | The agent builds in its own shell (R5.7, model B). The web's build writes `wwwroot` into the service's paths, `npm run quick` reads every room (`package.json:16`), `tools/contracts` compiles the contracts and the modules (`ContractsGen.csproj:19-20`), and laneless props and `canon/canon.json` feed every desktop build. So a room declares its `reads` and `writes`, and rooms whose closures meet are one claim group, one session in place each (R5.4). With the fast-forward holding a group at its base, a session's build is its room's work over that base. Repository-wide gates still read every room: said, and the landing's gates are the proof. Outputs in place are the checkout's, never cleared by Daoris; a session builds before it runs one. **Per-session outputs** (a session's `ArtifactsPath` or its toolchain's kin) are not row 2's answer: each session would build cold (730 files and 927 MB here, R5.2), the churn rooms end, and sources would still cross. Under groups, outputs per room are not needed. The lock per unit is withdrawn. **In this repository** the closures make one group beside the records, so in place serves one session at a time there and the rest take kept slots until B. §25's F2 sentence is corrected. Rows 1 and 2 |

(b) `pre-commit`, `prepare-commit-msg`, `commit-msg` and `post-commit` run with the private index named (`git hook run`), and a commit is signed where `commit.gpgSign` says; below git 2.36 no room is offered. ## 27. Round 7: rows 3 and 4, the kept slot made solid

Codex read rows 2 to 4 as round 6 left them, at `b347f3ab20`, verbatim in `docs/2026-10-26-tree-pool-reads.md` (*Codex, round 6*). It
asked another round for row 3 on its key (finding 8) and, narrowly, for row 4 on what a slot carries (10). In row 4's claim fencing,
named-job takeover, holder checks and non-forcing switch it found no work-loss case. The independent Opus reader read round 6's row 2 there
(*Opus (independent), round 6*), freed rows 3 and 4, and asked that whichever of rows 2 and 4 lands first build the census (F9). The owner
then chose that **the kept slot is made solid first** (D214's note of 2026-11-07, round 6's choices): rows 3 and 4 are built before row 2,
this repository keeps its sessions on kept slots, and row 2 waits for a later round. Round 7 answers rows 3 and 4 alone, each standing
without row 2, inside the owner's frame: the trees stay on the machine's NTFS volume and nothing is cloned; Daoris is generic, so the key
and the checks hold for any toolchain; and the purpose is to stop writing and deleting a whole repository per session. Where a toolchain's
state cannot be checked cheaply, the slot installs it again or does not carry it, and never trusts it. Every line the table cites was read at
main `a94a922745`. A passage round 7 changes is marked *(R7-Cn)* for Codex's finding n, *(R7-On)* for the Opus reader's Fn, *(R6-F9)* to
*(R6-F11a)* for the round-5 read's findings round 6 left to rows 3 and 4, and *(R7)* for round 7's own.

| Finding | Holds? | The change, and the row that proves it |
|---|---|---|
| Codex 8 (blocking) The dependency key identifies a lockfile, not the prepared install | **Holds. Taken: the key names the install, and only Daoris's own install fills the store** | **Holds.** This repository's root `npm ci` runs its `prepare` script (`package.json:14`), which runs `tools/merge-rows.mjs` and `tools/index-hook.mjs`, neither of them a lockfile. A gate's environment is composed by removal (`Review/Domain/GateEnvironment.cs:23-28`, `:66-75`), so every variable but Daoris's and git's reaches it, `NODE_ENV` and a registry's among them. The declaration already names the variables a gate is given (`Workplace/IntegrationDeclaration.cs:27`, `:209-216`). **The key** (R5.6) holds the declared `key`, each tracked path or glob at its blob id and an absent one as absent, and adds the `install` command, each `toolchain` command's output, the install's environment, the system, the architecture, the path and the repository. **The environment is composed by selection**: the system's own names and the dependency's `env`, each name and value keyed, nothing else inherited. The `key` is the repository's statement that no other tracked file changes the folder, accepted with the declaration (below). With no `key`, the commit's whole tree keys it, so it is shared only at one commit. **The fill** is Daoris's own run of `install` at a miss, in a tree whose tracked files stand at the commit, its folder cleared first, before any session works there. It seals nothing where the install changed a tracked file, wrote beside its folder anything but a declared output, left a link leading outside the tree, or wrote the tree's path into a file. **What no check proves** is an input an install reads only where it is present (a patch folder): the canon's conventions name each manager's, and a repository that cannot bound them declares no `key`. Entries are no longer shared across repositories. Row 3: a changed lifecycle script, `.npmrc` or `env` value at one lockfile each missing; a variable not named never reaching the install |
| Codex 9 Manifest tolerance must tell caches from added dependencies | **Holds. Taken as Codex proposes** | TREEDEPS1 leaves three named caches at a folder's top (`Workplace/TreeDependencies.cs:44-45`, `:248`), never any other addition, where round 5's read would have let "files added beside them" pass. **Each placement writes a placement record last**: its key, and a manifest of every file, folder and link in it (a link by its target within the tree), with each copy's size, write and change time and the store's hash. **At every take the namespace is read against it**, stat first and a hash where a stat differs. A file, folder or link missing, changed, or added outside the dependency's declared `caches` replaces the placement. A folder with no record is no placement. Copies are writable: `File.Copy` copies the read-only mark, so a placement clears it. Every removal of a placed folder takes its links as links first and never follows one (PRUNELINKS1's rule, `tools/worktree-remove.mjs:2-5`). Rows 3 and 4: an `npm install --no-save` of another package at an unchanged lockfile replaced at the next take; a file under `.vite/` kept |
| Codex 10 (blocking) Path stability alone cannot validate a carried environment | **Holds. Taken: every class a slot carries has a key and a check** | R5.5 defines an intermediate as what its build tool checks itself, yet lists a `.venv`, which a test run uses without anything reading its lockfile. No tree carries one today, every tree being fresh (`Workplace/TreeOpening.cs:133`); `Workplace/TreeScratch.cs:20` names `.venv` only for what a removal lets go. **A path-bound install is a dependency kept in place.** One the declaration marks `bound`, or whose fill finds the tree's path in its files, is never stored. A slot keeps it while its key and placement record hold, and installs it again in place before the session starts when either moves. A failed install leaves it absent, said. **Intermediates are keyed by the tree's `toolchain`**, the commands whose output keys them with the system and the architecture. When it moves they are renewed: moved aside as at the size bound, never deleted at a take. **A folder a runner uses without building is an output**, whatever tool checks it: a CMake build tree, whose executables `ctest` runs as they stand, unless every runner builds first. R5.5's table and examples and the canon's `rooms-and-classes` are corrected. Row 4: a `.venv` kept at its key, and installed again after a `pip install` into it; `obj/` renewed after an SDK change |

What it reads, `HEAD` and the whole status (`Participants/Helpers/HelperCapture.cs:99-104`), is the asker's own in a slot: a slot holds one line's work, and the helper wait holds the slot (§5.2 step 1). The census lists the capture with the readers that open a record's tree, each with what it reads there and why that is its line's own in a slot, and row c re-keys it to `WhereIs`. The semantics Codex asks for are a room's, and row 2's. Row 4 (its rows a and c) |

A slot's folder is no record's path, so a running line's slot reads *not in use*. **Row 3, as it is built** *(R7)*.
- **Changes.**

    the placement: a copy eight at once, writable, its links made again inside the tree, its record written last. It also holds the

    TREEDEPS1 copies now. At a miss they run its `install` as a stage after the open and before the agent starts, outside the start's

- **The person sees.** At a session's start, a line for each declared dependency: placed from the store, installed before the session
  began, installed in each tree since it names its folder, or not placed and why (its declaration awaiting acceptance, its install failed in
  its own words). The Disk space card shows a `deps` kind. The first start after a key moves on this machine starts later, by an install.
- **Proof** (`TreeDepsTests`, `TreeDepsGitTests` and a Windows `Process` class):
  - **the key**: at one lockfile, a changed lifecycle script, a changed `.npmrc`, a changed `env` value, another toolchain output and
    another repository each miss; a variable no `env` names never reaches the install; with no `key`, a hit only at one commit; a `key`
    glob newly matching a file (`patches/`) moves the key;
  - **the fill**: an entry sealed only after Daoris's own `install` in a tree no session worked in, and nothing sealed from a tree whose
    session ran `npm install`; an install that changes a tracked file, writes an ignored path beside its folder that no output names,
    leaves a link outside the tree or writes the tree's path into a file, each sealing nothing and saying which; a pnpm install whose
    files are hard links into pnpm's own store sealed by copy, that store's files left writable;
  - **the placement**: by copy and writable, its workspace links made again and leading inside the tree; a removal of a placed folder
    holding a junction to the checkout's sources leaving those sources whole (PRUNELINKS1's case);
  - **the record**: a placed file edited, removed, or added outside a declared cache failing, and one added under a declared cache passing;
    a folder with no record read as no placement;
  - **refusals**: an install failing or past its bound leaving the folder absent, said, and the session starting; a declaration unaccepted,
    or changed since its acceptance, placing and running nothing, said;
  - **eviction** by days and by size, a placed copy unaffected;
  - **TREEDEPS1 gone**: no `started.uncopied-*` code written, TREEGONE1's remake placing from the store, and `SessionTreesTests`,
    `TreeReturnsTests` and the tick tests that open real trees whole;
  - **measured, in the row's note**: this repository's two folders sealed, or the file that names its tree; a fresh tree at an unchanged
    key placed, against `npm ci`; a fill's time and bytes.
